# Pre-registration — Project 1

**Frozen:** 2026-10-23, before any model was trained. Git tag `prereg-v1`.
Any later change goes in §9 (Deviations) with date and reason; nothing above §9 is edited after the tag.

**Study:** Leak-free re-evaluation of ARLHNIDS-IoT (Srivastava & Sinha, Computers & Security 156, 2025, 104515) and a tree-distilled int8 MLP that does known-class classification and unknown-family detection from one forward pass.

---

## 1. Research questions and hypotheses

| ID | Hypothesis | Decision rule |
|---|---|---|
| H0 | The paper's reported metrics are inflated by its evaluation protocol. | Measurement, no pass/fail. Report Δ (paper-order − leak-free) per metric, per dataset, with 95% bootstrap CI, and attribute Δ to each protocol step (§4). |
| H1 | The distilled student is close to its teacher on known classes. | Primary KD student (S6) macro-F1 ≥ teacher macro-F1 − 2.0 pts, mean over families × seeds. |
| H2 | int8 post-training quantisation does not change unknown-family detection. | \|AUROC(int8) − AUROC(fp32)\| ≤ 1 std of AUROC across seeds, for energy and Mahalanobis, on every dataset. |
| H3 | KD transfers useful uncertainty: the KD student's unknown score beats no-teacher students and the teacher's own score. | Primary metric M1. S6 vs S1, S6 vs S3, S6 vs teacher vote entropy; paired one-sided Wilcoxon over (family, seed), Holm-corrected α = 0.05, for each of energy and Mahalanobis. |
| H4 | The paper's DDQN (re-implemented leak-free) is not better than a plain classifier + score. | Exploratory, two-sided, no prior direction. |

## 2. Data

| Key | Source | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `unsw_official` | UNSW-NB15 official train/test (175,341 / 82,332) | Paper reproduction |
| `unsw_raw` | UNSW-NB15_1..4 (≈2.54M) with timestamps | Leak-free + LOFO |
| `bot_iot` | BoT-IoT 5% "10-best" train/test (2,934,817 / 733,705) | Paper reproduction + leak-free + LOFO |
| `ciciot_paper` | 8 capture files from CIC 2024 CSV release, exactly as ARLHNIDS Table 4 (401,260 rows) | Paper reproduction |
| `ciciot_csv` | All per-pcap CSVs in the same release (34 classes) | Leak-free + LOFO (primary CICIoT set) |
| `ciciot_merged` | Original CICIoT2023 MERGED_CSV (46 features) | Duplicate-rate audit; secondary leak-free |

Loader row counts are asserted against the paper's tables (`tests/test_load.py`).

**Observation recorded before training:** ARLHNIDS's CICIoT2023 subset uses exactly one capture file per class. Class identity is therefore confounded with capture session; no grouped split is possible on that subset.

## 3. Cleaning (identical for every leak-free experiment)

1. Drop rows with NaN or ±inf in any feature. Count them.
2. Drop identifier / known-leak columns:
   - UNSW: `id`, `srcip`, `sport`, `dstip`, `dsport`, `stime`, `ltime`, `sttl`, `dttl`, `ct_state_ttl`, `label`.
   - BoT-IoT: `pkSeqID`, `saddr`, `sport`, `daddr`, `dport`, `seq`, `attack`, `subcategory`.
   - CICIoT: `Number`; `Weight` also dropped if it is a deterministic function of `Number` (checked on training data only, result logged).
   The paper-order reproduction keeps whatever the paper keeps.
3. Hash each row over the retained features. Drop every row whose hash carries more than one label. Then drop exact duplicates (keep first). Report counts per dataset and per class.

## 4. Splits

Seeds {0, 1, 2, 3, 4} for every stochastic step.

| Dataset | Leak-free split (train / val / test = 60 / 20 / 20) |
|---|---|
| `ciciot_csv` | `StratifiedGroupKFold`, group = capture file |
| `unsw_raw` | `StratifiedGroupKFold`, group = 1-hour window of `stime` |
| `bot_iot` | `StratifiedGroupKFold`, group = block of 10,000 consecutive `pkSeqID` (time proxy) |
| `unsw_official`, `ciciot_paper`, `ciciot_merged` | split first, stratified random (no group id available — stated as a limitation) |

All fitted transforms (imputer, clamp quantiles, scaler, balancer, feature selector) see training rows only. Synthetic rows never enter val or test. Hyperparameters are chosen on val only.

**H0 attribution:** starting from the paper's order, switch one step at a time to leak-free and record Δ: (a) merge-then-split → split-first, (b) oversample-then-split → split-then-oversample-train, (c) Optuna on test → Optuna on val, (d) dedup off → on, (e) DoS-held-out with DDoS in train → Flood held out.

## 5. Models

**Teacher:** majority VotingClassifier of DT, RF, ExtraTrees, AdaBoost, LightGBM, CatBoost, XGBoost (paper's seven). Optuna TPE, 50 trials per model, objective = val macro-F1, sampler seed = run seed. Soft labels = mean predicted probability, produced **5-fold out-of-fold** on the training set.

**Students:** MLP, input → 64 → 32 → classes, ReLU, Adam lr 1e-3, batch 512, max 50 epochs, early stopping on val loss (patience 5).

| Arm | Training signal |
|---|---|
| S1 | cross-entropy (CE) |
| S2 | CE, then post-hoc temperature scaling on val |
| S3 | CE with label smoothing 0.1 |
| S4 | KD, T = 1, α = 1 |
| S5 | KD, T = 4, α = 1 |
| **S6 (primary)** | KD, T = 4, α = 0.5 + CE on hard labels |

int8: PyTorch → TFLite full-integer post-training quantisation, calibration = 1,000 random training rows (seeded).

## 6. Unknown-family protocol (LOFO)

Families (DoS + DDoS merged):
- `ciciot_csv`: Flood, Recon, Spoofing, Web, BruteForce, Mirai
- `unsw_raw`: DoS, Exploits, Fuzzers, Generic, Recon, Analysis+Backdoor, Shellcode+Worms
- `bot_iot`: Flood, Recon, Theft (Theft n = 79 → reported, flagged low-n, excluded from H-tests)

Per round: every row of the held-out family is removed from train and val and appears only in test. Benign is present in every round.

**Scores:** energy (T = 1), Mahalanobis (penultimate layer, class means + tied covariance fitted on train each round), kNN (k = 10, penultimate, cosine), MSP.
**Baselines:** teacher vote entropy (primary tree comparator), teacher top-2 probability margin, calibrated RF max-prob, IsolationForest on benign train, leak-free DDQN (paper's AIDS).

## 7. Metrics

| ID | Metric |
|---|---|
| **M1 (primary)** | AUROC of the unknown score: held-out family (positive) vs benign test (negative) |
| M2 | Held-out-family recall under the combined rule (predicted class ≠ benign OR score > τ), τ = 99th percentile of benign-val scores |
| M3 | FPR at 95% TPR |
| M4 | Alarm precision at attack base rates 1% and 0.1% (Bayes from M2's TPR and benign FPR) |
| M5 | OOD AUROC: held-out family vs all in-distribution test rows |
| K1 | Known-class macro-F1 on test (held-out family excluded) |

Reporting: mean ± 95% cluster-bootstrap CI (10,000 resamples over families). Every reported F1 is recomputed from P and R as a consistency check.

## 8. Exclusions and stopping

- A run is excluded only for a crash or a failing leak test (§ tests). Excluded runs are listed, not dropped silently.
- No extra seeds, families or arms are added after seeing test results for H1–H3; additions are labelled exploratory.

|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-23 | §4 | For grouped splits, the seed selects the fold (test = fold `seed % 5` of a fixed `StratifiedGroupKFold(5)`, val = fold `seed % 4` of the inner `StratifiedGroupKFold(4)`) instead of seeding the shuffle. Seeds 0–4 therefore form 5-fold cross-validation over groups. | sklearn's `StratifiedGroupKFold(shuffle=True)` only shuffles ties, so different seeds gave identical splits (caught by `tests/test_split.py`). Decided before any model was trained. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4 | `bot_iot` uses the stratified random split (after dedup) instead of `pkSeqID` blocks. | `pkSeqID` in the 5% / 10-best files is ordered by class (DoS 1–1,650,260, DDoS, Normal, Reconnaissance, Theft), not by time, so the blocks grouped by label: all Normal rows fell in one block and none reached test. The files carry no timestamp or capture id. Found by `scripts/audit_clean.py` before any model was trained. |
| 2026-10-23 | §2, §3 | `unsw_raw`: blank (empty or whitespace) `ct_flw_http_mthd`, `is_ftp_login`, `ct_ftp_cmd` are read as 0, not dropped as missing. | Blank means "not applicable" (non-HTTP / non-FTP flow); UNSW's own train/test sets store these as 0. Treating them as missing dropped 57% of rows. Found by the audit before any model was trained. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4 | `unsw_raw` groups are 10-minute `stime` windows, not 1-hour. | Hourly windows gave 27 groups (16 with attacks); SGKF put every attack in train and none in test. 10-minute windows give 149 groups, each attack family in ≥ 82. Found by the audit before any model was trained. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4 | `ciciot_csv` groups are contiguous tenths of each capture file, not whole files. | BruteForce has 1 capture file, Spoofing 3, Benign 4, Recon 5, so whole-file groups put those families entirely in train. Contiguous blocks keep temporally adjacent flows together while giving every family ≥ 10 groups. Found by the audit before any model was trained. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4 (H0 attribution) | Added step (f): BoT-IoT label columns `attack` and `subcategory` kept as features (paper order) → dropped. | The paper's Table 11 gives BoT-IoT 18 feature dimensions, which equals the 10-best file's 19 columns minus the target `category`, so the paper's features include the binary attack label and the attack subcategory. Found while reading the method before any ARLHNIDS run. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4, §5 (reproduction details the paper does not report) | FCM uses the paper's Table 8 settings instead of re-running Optuna (its objective is not reported); centroids are fit on a 50k-row sample per class; the reduced class keeps Table 9's size, drawn from every cluster in proportion to its size ("Grouping" is not described). GWO uses Table 11 (wolves, iterations, seed 40, α = 0.5, correlation < 0.6) with fitness from a 50-tree XGBoost on a 20k-row stratified training sample (75/25 internal holdout), run for Table 11's fixed iteration counts. SIDS uses the paper's Table 13 settings; ET `max_features="auto"` = `"sqrt"`, AdaBoost runs SAMME (SAMME.R was removed from scikit-learn), and the hard vote is computed by hand so XGBoost can early-stop on its eval set (test in paper order, val in leak-free order). Until the modified GAMO is built, SMOTE oversamples minority classes; class targets keep Table 10's per-class ratios, so paper order reproduces Table 10 exactly. | Needed to run the paper's pipeline at all; each choice is the closest reading of the paper and is applied identically in paper order and leak-free order, so it cannot create the paper-vs-leak-free gap. Decided before any ARLHNIDS run. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4, §5 (modified GAMO details the paper does not report) | GAMO follows Table 6 and the §3.4 text: noise length 32, conditional feature mapping unit, one convex SGU per oversampled class whose softmax weights (text §3.4.3; Table 6 puts the softmax one layer earlier) mix that class's real training rows, conditional discriminator, classifier M. Each "Dense n softmax" + "Attention n" pair of Table 6 is read as one soft feature gate, x[:n]·softmax(Wx+b), because the paper does not define attention; the literal reading (a softmax layer on the input) collapsed every class to a single synthetic row on UNSW. Only the classes that are not undersampled enter GAMO; 75% of each is the training set and convex basis (matching Table 7, e.g. UNSW Worms 130 of 174), and 25% scores M. Training budget: 12,000 Adam steps (3,000 left M at 43% holdout accuracy on UNSW), batch 256, lr 2e-4, betas (0.5, 0.999); fake classes are drawn in proportion to the rows still needed (the (P_c − P_i) weights of Eqs. 9 and 12); the generator's classifier term is −log(1 − M_i), the bounded form of Eq. 9. Per-class targets are unchanged (Table 10). Partial mode collapse remains for some classes (e.g. UNSW Worms); each run records per-class diversity (spread vs real, nearest-real distance, unique share) instead of adding a diversity term the paper does not have. SMOTE stays as the control arm. GWO fitness evaluations now run in parallel, one single-threaded XGBoost per wolf; the search is unchanged. | The paper gives the architecture but no attention definition, epochs, batch size or learning rate. The settings are fixed before any GAMO result and applied identically in both orders. |
| 2026-10-23 | §4 (H0 attribution) | Added step (g): BoT-IoT identifier columns (`pkSeqID`, `saddr`, `sport`, `daddr`, `dport`, `seq`) kept as features (paper order) → dropped. Seed-0 runs also report, in paper order, accuracy on real vs synthetic test rows and the share of test rows that exactly match a training row. | Added after the seed-0 paper-order BoT-IoT run, where GWO kept `pkSeqID` alone and every model but AdaBoost scored ≥ 99.5% on it (`pkSeqID` follows class order; see the `bot_iot` split row above). Labelled post hoc: it is an attribution step, not a change to H1–H3. |
| 2026-10-24 | §1, §5, §6 (new H5, post hoc) | **H5 — Rehearsed Budgeted Fusion.** Added after the exploratory LOFO pilot (seed 0, `scripts/pilot_lofo.py`), where the KD student lost to RF/XGB `not_benign` on unseen families (H3 direction failed). Members: RF, XGB and MLP-CE `not_benign` (supervised block); IsolationForest on benign train and global-reference benign-cluster distance (benign-deviation block). Each member's score is mapped to a tail p-value `p = (1 + #{ref ≥ x}) / (n + 1)` against `benign_rank`; fused score `S = −min_m p_m / share_m`; threshold = higher empirical (1 − FPR)-quantile of `S` on `benign_cal`, applied with strict `>`. One free constant `alpha_sup` (supervised block share; the rest goes to the deviation block; equal split inside each block), grid 0.30–1.00 step 0.05, chosen by rehearsal (row below). **Win:** macro TPR@1%FPR over eligible families beats both XGB and faithful MCDE, 5-seed CI excluding 0, and no eligible family worse than either by more than 0.03. Reported per family. | The pilot showed tail complementarity on CICIoT (MLP-CE TPR@1%FPR Flood 1.00, BruteForce 0.41 vs RF 0.37/0.16). Budgeted p-value fusion is prior art (MCDE, Yue et al., Entropy 28(9):1026, 2026); what H5 adds is rehearsal-chosen shares, the multi-member supervised block and evaluation on the hard families. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6 (H5 rehearsal) | For outer fold k, `alpha_sup` is chosen on pair models trained on benign + F∖{k, j} (each with its own preprocessing and its own early-stopping split), for every inner family j ≠ k. Each pair model scores j's **train + val** rows as pseudo-unknowns; test rows never enter alpha selection. Objective: macro TPR@1%FPR over eligible inner families. Alphas within max(0.005, paired SE) of the best are tied, paired SE = std over inner families of (TPR_α − TPR_best) / √F; ties are broken by the highest harmonic mean of (TPR + 0.05), then the alpha closest to 0.9. If the whole curve lies inside the tolerance, `flat = True` is reported (the share does not matter). Pair models are cached by the unordered set {k, j}. | One tuned constant, because flexible combiners fit on few held-out domains overfit (leave-one-domain-out selection in DomainBed; outlier-set overfitting in OD-test). Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6, §7 (gate protocol (a)) | Decisions use rehearsal (inner) families only. **Stage 2 ceiling gate:** for each eligible inner family j, `headroom_vs_trees` (best of Bonferroni union and recalibrated equal fusion, minus the best of RF and XGB, from `union_ceiling_check`) is averaged over the outer folds where j is inner, then a 5-seed t-CI is taken. Go if the macro CI over eligible inner families is > 0, or if at least one family has headroom > 0 on ≥ 4 of 5 seeds; otherwise fusion is reported as feature-capped for that dataset. **Stage 3 abort gate:** fusion is abandoned on a dataset if no member's (TPR@1%FPR − max(RF, XGB)) on any eligible inner family has a 5-seed CI > 0. Outer-family ceiling and seed tables are benchmark diagnostics and are never used for a decision. With many families × members some comparisons pass by chance; the seed-consistency rule is the guard against that. | Keeps every go/no-go decision off the test rows of the held-out family. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §2, §7 (datasets and operating points) | `unsw_raw` (grouped `stime//600` split) is the primary UNSW dataset of the tail benchmark; `unsw_official` (stratified random split) is secondary. `ciciot_csv` is primary. BoT-IoT is reported by AUROC only (477 benign rows in the whole file, so a 1% threshold has no support). Fixed FPR set {0.1%, 1%, 5%}; the 0.1% column only where n_cal ≥ 10,000 (unsw_raw, ciciot_csv; not unsw_official). α_min: 0.1% for unsw_raw and ciciot_csv, 1% for unsw_official. Every threshold asserts n_cal ≥ 10 / FPR. Hard gate: calibration-set FPR ≤ target (holds by construction; asserted). Flag only: realised benign-test FPR, per score, family and dataset. pAUC is the standardized partial AUC over FPR ∈ [0, 1%] (McClish 1989; 0.5 = chance, 1 = perfect). Families with fewer than 300 rows scored in a role are reported but excluded from every macro objective and gate, identically for all methods, inside rehearsal too. | A random split leaves near-duplicate benign rows in val and test, which flatters the realised FPR; unsw_raw's grouped split does not. Budgeted fusion gives a deviation member 0.05% of the budget at alpha_sup = 0.9, so its p-values must resolve ~5e-4, which needs ~20k reference rows. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §4, §5 (benign roles, training cap, early stopping) | Benign val is split 50/50 into `benign_rank` and `benign_cal` by group block (random on unsw_official). Benign val, rank, cal and test are uncapped. Training is capped at 40,000 rows per family, drawn once per seed by proportional allocation across training blocks (within-group random rank, never the first N rows); every outer and pair model uses the same rows of each family. IsolationForest and the k-means members use a fixed 200,000-row benign-train sample per seed. No member uses `benign_rank` or `benign_cal` for anything: early stopping, eval sets, hyperparameters or the choice of K. Early stopping uses a 10% group-carved split of each model's own training rows. Benign rows are reused across outer and inner configurations; benign is always a known class, so this is not leakage. | The pilot MLP early-stopped on val, which includes the benign rows that set the threshold; the pilot stays exploratory. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6 (MCDE baseline) | Faithful MCDE (Yue et al. 2026, §3.2–§4.2): supervised branch = HistGradientBoosting (binary benign vs known attack; sklearn defaults, the paper gives no hyperparameters); benign branch = median imputation, signed log, robust scaling to the 5th–95th percentile range, fitted on benign train; mini-batch k-means, K = 15, on benign train; RMS diagonal Mahalanobis distance to the nearest mode; global scale floored per coordinate at max(1e-3, 5% of the median global std among coordinates > 1e-6) (1e-3 if none); per-mode scale floored at 5% of the floored global scale; modes with fewer than 20 training vectors use the global scale. Tail probabilities u = (1 + #{ref ≥ x}) / (n + 1); supervised reference and mode-conditioned distance references come from MCDE's reference set = our `benign_rank`; a mode with fewer than n_min = ⌈5 / α_min⌉ reference rows uses the pooled reference (5,000 at α_min = 0.1%, 500 at 1%). `S = −min(u_sup / w, u_mode / (1 − w))`, w = 0.9; threshold = higher (1 − α) quantile on MCDE's threshold set = our `benign_cal`, strict `>`. Inside H5, the cluster-distance member uses the global reference; each table states which reference it uses. | Checked against the full text before freezing. The paper's CICIoT2023 stress test held out DDoS, DoS and Mirai one at a time with the other two in training; our joint hold-out (row below) measures that setting. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6 (extra CICIoT hold-outs) | In addition to the six families: (i) Flood + Mirai held out jointly (unseen-family; inner families BruteForce, Recon, Spoofing and Web only, so alpha is tuned on hard families, reported as such); (ii) DoS alone and (iii) DDoS alone, labelled **partial-family (attack-type) hold-outs**, not unseen-family results, because the sibling type stays in training. All three are rehearsed like the main folds. The Flood extrapolation check is reported: share of Flood test rows outside the training range on the top features, and whether RF/XGB scores saturate where the MLP score keeps rising. | `cic_family` merges DDoS and DoS into Flood while Mirai's floods (greeth, greip, udpplain) stay in training when Flood is held out, which may make Flood look easy. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §3, §6 (unsw_raw `ct_*` features) | The creators computed the windowed `ct_*` features over the last 100 connections of all flows, attacks included, so training rows can encode the held-out family's flows. Before any full run we measure, per held-out family, the share of training rows and of benign-test rows whose window holds that family's flows, plus the share of windows crossing a `stime//600` block. If both shares are below 0.1% for every family, the original `ct_*` stay in the main variant and our causal `ct_*` are a robustness check; otherwise our causal `ct_*` are the main variant. Both are reported, with `ct_*` dropped as a third variant. Our causal `ct_*` recompute the 7 windowed features (`ct_src_ltm`, `ct_dst_ltm`, `ct_src_dport_ltm`, `ct_dst_sport_ltm`, `ct_dst_src_ltm`, `ct_srv_src`, `ct_srv_dst`); `ct_flw_http_mthd` and `ct_ftp_cmd` are per-flow and unchanged; `ct_state_ttl` stays dropped. Window = the smallest run of whole seconds back from t holding ≥ 100 flows, inside the row's own block, with the current second included in full (a 1-second decision latency); `stime` has 1-second resolution (96.6% of rows share a second) and the file is only 85–88% time-sorted, so file order is not used and the exposure count is approximate. Removal sets by role: for outer fold k, every row used to fit, early-stop, rank or calibrate is computed with k's flows removed, and only final test scoring (benign test plus k) uses all flows; for pair {k, j} in fold k's rehearsal, fit, early-stopping, rank and cal rows remove k and j, and j's pseudo-unknown rows remove k and keep j (fold j is the mirror). Windows are cached by removal set. A benign-test FPR above the calibration FPR is reported per family as the false-alarm cost of an unknown attack being present, never recalibrated away. | Same machinery as the per-host window features of Idea 2, built once. Fixed before any score-bank run. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6 (MCDE full-text check, before Stages 4–5) | Checked against the full text (§3.2–§4.2.2): K = 15 (main configuration and the CICIoT2023 runs), w = 0.9, scale floor max(10⁻³, 5% of the median global std among coordinates > 10⁻⁶), local floor 5% of the floored global scale, modes < 20 training vectors use the global scale, u = (1 + #{ref ≥ x}) / (n + 1), n_min = ⌈5 / α_min⌉ with pooled fallback, higher-quantile threshold with strict `>` on a disjoint benign set: all as implemented. One deviation found and fixed: the paper mode-imputes and one-hot encodes categorical fields with block weight 0.5, while `mcde.py` label-encoded them. MCDE now has its own one-hot preprocessing for both branches and its own benign modes (`mcde_sup`, `mcde_dist`, `mcde_mode`); our cluster-distance and IsolationForest members keep label encoding. This changes only unsw_raw / unsw_official (`proto`, `state`, `service`); CICIoT has no categorical features. The existing outer banks are patched by refitting only the MCDE branches (the refit label-encoded cluster distance reproduces the stored column exactly, which checks that the rows and fits are identical). The paper gives no HGB hyperparameters (sklearn defaults kept) and no mini-batch k-means settings (batch 4,096, n_init 3 kept). | Faithful comparator for H5. Fixed before any Stage 4–5 number. |
| 2026-10-24 | §7 (Stage 2 gate as run) | The Stage 2 gate was decided on equal shares (best of the Bonferroni union and the recalibrated equal-share fusion); no best-alpha amendment was applied. Before Stage 5 a best-alpha headroom is reported as a diagnostic only: (i) per (fold, inner family) the best alpha on the grid (an oracle upper bound) and (ii) per fold the alpha with the best macro over that fold's inner families. Neither changes the gate decision. | Answers whether equal shares understated the headroom. |
| 2026-10-24 | §6, §7 (H5: does fusion add anything) | Two extra comparisons, reported next to the registered H5 test, which is unchanged (beat XGB and faithful MCDE): (a) MLP-CE alone; (b) a rehearsal-selected single member: for each outer fold k, the fusion member (RF, XGB, MLP-CE, IsolationForest, cluster distance) with the best macro TPR@1%FPR over fold k's eligible inner families, scored by the same pair models used for alpha, with the same tie tolerance (max(0.005, paired SE)), then the highest harmonic mean of (TPR + 0.05), then the order RF, XGB, MLP-CE, IsolationForest, cluster distance. Fusion counts as adding something only if it beats both (a) and (b) under the same CI rule as H5. | The member tables show MLP-CE alone carries much of the CICIoT gain; fusion has to beat picking one member the same leak-free way. |
| 2026-10-24 | §7 (Stage 5 protocol) | Alpha is chosen per outer fold with `rehearse_alpha` at 1% FPR (deviation block = IsolationForest and global-reference cluster distance); the same alpha is applied at 0.1% and 5%. H5 macro = mean over the eligible main outer families (≥ 300 test rows; the extra CICIoT hold-outs are reported separately, each with its label). The H5 CI is the wider of the 5-seed t-CI and a two-level bootstrap: 2,000 draws, seeds resampled with replacement, then within each seed the unknown test rows of each family and the benign cal rows resampled, thresholds recomputed, paired across methods; percentile 95% interval. The "no eligible family worse by more than 0.03" rule uses the 5-seed mean. | Fixed before any Stage 5 number. |
| 2026-10-24 | §3, §6 (unsw_raw `ct_*` variants in Stage 5) | Stage 5 runs on three variants: original `ct_*`; causal `ct_*` over all flows for every role (`ct_causal_all`, new: attack flows stay in every window); causal `ct_*` with per-role removal (`ct_causal`). By the main-variant rule (exposure 5–50%), `ct_causal` is the headline. `ct_*` dropped stays outer-only and is used to check whether IsolationForest's Generic advantage holds when RF no longer depends on `ct_*`. | Separates what the windows leak from what causality costs. |
| 2026-10-24 | §3 (unsw_raw leak-column controls) | Diagnostic controls under the same LOFO folds, rows and cal thresholds: an RF (100 trees) on the dropped TTL columns only (`sttl`, `dttl`, `ct_state_ttl`), an RF on the IP addresses only (`srcip`, `dstip` as 32-bit integers), and a depth-unlimited decision tree (min leaf 50) on every single feature (original and causal `ct_*` included), TPR@1%FPR on the held-out family. The best single feature is chosen on the test rows, so it is an oracle upper bound. | 2026-10-24 | §5, §6 (P1: closed-leaf / leaf-local residual hypothesis; post hoc, added after Stage 5; written before any P1 run) | **Status of fusion:** the H5 fusion stays as a baseline and benchmark result, not as our method. **Hypothesis.** RF and XGB stop splitting once a node is pure benign, so a benign leaf is bounded only on the features its path split on and stays open on the others. Unseen families that fall on the benign side of those splits land in such leaves while lying far from that leaf's benign rows on features the path never constrained. This would explain why, on CICIoT Flood (all held-out rows inside the training range), a benign-deviation score (global cluster distance, TPR@1%FPR 0.890) beats RF (0.346) and XGB (0.677); IsolationForest (0.655) does not. **Extension.** Inside a benign leaf features co-vary; an unseen attack can be normal on every single feature yet break those couplings, so a residual against the leaf's own benign subspace (r ≥ 1) would catch what the per-feature score (r = 0, the FERL case; Fumanal-Idocin & Andreu-Perez, arXiv 2608.05859) misses. Prior art: MCDE (budgeted fusion), FERL (per-node diagonal Gaussian), Lakhina et al. 2004 and mixtures of PPCA (residual subspace), CADE and Vyas et al. 2018 (leave-one-family-out rehearsal). **Step 1 diagnostic** (CICIoT outer folds Flood, BruteForce, Recon, Spoofing, Web; 5 seeds): RF and XGB refit with the bank's seed, rows and preprocessing; the refit must reproduce the stored bank scores, otherwise stop. Leaf statistics from the 200k benign-train sample (`bsample`) only; known-attack counts from the model's own attack training rows; asserted disjoint from `benign_rank` / `benign_cal` and added to the manifest leak test. A leaf is benign-dominated if < 1% of its training rows (benign sample + attack fit rows) are known attacks. Per-feature leaf scale = q90 − q10 floored at 5% of the global q90 − q10; z = abs(x − leaf median) / scale. Path features are classified as bounded both sides, half-open or (not on the path) unsplit. For speed the diagnostic uses a fixed random 100 trees per model (RF: of 300; XGB: of 300 × classes); benign test rows are subsampled to 50,000 (fixed seed). **Stop rule (fixed now):** the mechanism is wrong and steps 2–4 are not built unless, for both Flood and BruteForce, (a) on both RF and XGB the majority (> 50%) of held-out rows' tree hits land in benign-dominated leaves, and (b) inside benign-dominated leaves with ≥ 20 benign rows that also hold benign test hits, the unsplit-feature deviation of attack rows is clearly larger than that of benign test rows: within-leaf AUROC ≥ 0.70 and median ratio ≥ 2 for at least one aggregate (max, top-3 mean, RSS), on both models. **Later steps (only if step 1 passes):** r ≥ 1 kept only if it beats r = 0 on the diagnostic ratio and on inner-family TPR@1%; aggregate, r and γ chosen on inner rehearsal families only (paired-SE tolerance, then harmonic mean, then lower r, then γ closest to the value equalizing the two terms' benign-train 99th percentiles). **Method success:** beats XGB, MCDE and MLP-CE on macro TPR@1%FPR over eligible outer families, CI (wider of 5-seed t-CI and two-level bootstrap) excluding 0, no family worse than any of the three by more than 0.03. All P1 results are labelled post hoc. | A contribution beyond stacking standard members needs a mechanism that a diagnostic confirms first. |

| 2026-10-24 | §6 (P1 replacement method: Conditional-Residual Network, CRN; written before any CRN or diagnostic number) | **Closed-leaf is closed** (step 1 FAIL, a7ef47c). **Method.** One network, one forward pass: G feature-group encoders e_g, H = Σ_g e_g(x_g), leave-one-group-out codes H₋g = H − e_g(x_g); head 1 = softmax over benign + K known families; head 2 = benign-only heteroscedastic Gaussian prediction of each group from H₋g (and, on datasets with hosts and timestamps, causal per-host context c), ℓ_g = Σ_{j∈g} (x_j − μ_j)²/(2 max(σ_j, σ_min)²) + log σ_j. Loss = CE + λ·E_benign[Σ_g ℓ_g] − β·E_known-attack[log(1 − exp(−ℓ̄/T))] (bounded push, Yamanaka et al. 2019). Novelty N = max_g (ℓ_g − m_g)/s_g with m, s from benign training rows; attack evidence A = log Σ_{k≥1} π_k − log π_0; decision = min of the two tail p-values against benign_rank, threshold on benign_cal (as all other scores); a flagged flow gets a known-family label if its conformal class set is a single known family, else "unknown". Prior art: MCM (ICLR 2024), DRL (ICLR 2025), NPT-AD (ICML 2024), QCAD, ABC, DRA; claimed delta = the combination (supervised head + benign conditional head in one pass, sum-minus-self, bounded push on the conditional residual, causal identity-free host context). **Gate D1 (no training).** Per outer fold, a Gaussian conditional residual on Gaussian-quantile-transformed features fitted on the benign-train sample only (never benign_rank / benign_cal): Ledoit-Wolf precision Λ, r_j = (Λz)_j/√Λ_jj, s_G = max_j |r_j|; reported with global Mahalanobis zᵀΛz. PASS if s_G (alone or rank-min with MLP-CE) improves TPR@1%FPR by ≥ 0.03 over the best existing single member (rf, xgb, mlp_ce, iforest, clust, mcde; 5-seed mean) on ≥ 2 hard families, where hard = {BruteForce, Recon, Spoofing, Web} on CICIoT and families whose best member is < 0.5 on unsw_raw (ct_causal), AND rank-min(s_G, MLP-CE) macro on CICIoT > 0.485. If D1 fails, one pre-declared second shot: per-feature LightGBM quantile regressors (q = 0.01, 0.99) on the same benign rows, QCAD-style score, same PASS rule. If both fail, CRN's within-row head is dropped and we move to the runner-up (reference-class softmax: K + 2 classes, extra class = column-shuffled benign, score = log p_ref − log p_0), same gate. **Gate D2 (headroom).** Seen-family ceiling: XGB fitted on all families (removal set ∅, same capped rows), TPR@1%FPR on each family's test rows at the benign_cal threshold. **Tuning.** CRN hyperparameters (G grouping, λ, β, T, width) are chosen only on inner rehearsal families (pair configs) or the model's own early-stopping split, never on outer test families; the grid and the chosen values are logged before outer scoring. **Data.** NetFlow-v3 (NF-UNSW-NB15-v3, NF-ToN-IoT-v3, NF-CSE-CIC-IDS2018-v3; Luay et al. 2025) are added as primary deployable datasets (standard nProbe NetFlow features with IPs and timestamps) under the same grouped LOFO protocol; CICIoT2023 is kept as a benchmark only because its features carry a class-dependent window artifact (Number = 10 for Benign / BruteForce / Recon / Spoofing / Web and 100 for Flood / Mirai on ≥ 99% of rows; indicator averages on 0.1 vs 0.01 grids; measured 2026-10-24). | The only measured positive signal (leaf-local > global conditional deviation on Spoofing / Web) motivates a conditional head that every flow reaches. |

| 2026-10-24 | §6, §7 (per-host conformal CUSUM kill test; written before any host-stream result) | **Question.** Does accumulating per-flow evidence along each source host's flow stream raise unseen-family detection at a fixed host-level alarm budget, beyond per-flow scoring and beyond simple aggregation? **Data.** nf_cic seed-0 banks (none variant primary, host variant secondary; seeds 1–4 not built). Score: mlp_ce (the deployable tiny model) primary, xgb secondary. Test attack rows are the bank's block-even subsample (cap 20k per family), so attacker streams are thinned while benign streams are complete: conservative for every aggregator. **Units.** Key = IPV4_SRC_ADDR (never a model input); stream = a host's rows in time order, reset at each UTC day; unit = host-day. Benign hosts = source IPs with no attack flow anywhere in the cleaned data, split 50/50 by a seeded hash into calibration hosts and test hosts (disjoint). Conformal p-value u = (1 + #{rank-role benign of calibration hosts with score ≥ s}) / (n + 1). Threshold h of each method = smallest value with ≤ 1% of calibration host-days (cal-role rows of calibration hosts) alarming. FPR = share of test-host benign host-days (test_benign rows) with ≥ 1 alarm. Attacker host-day = (host, day) with ≥ 1 held-out-family test flow; stream = all test rows of that host that day; TPR = share detected (≥ 1 alarm on a held-out-family flow); flow-level TPR (share of held-out flows at which the statistic ≥ h) reported too. Clean-history subset: attacker host-days whose host has no other attack family's flow in the cleaned data on that day or the day before. **Methods.** P per-flow (statistic = score); E EWMA of q = −log u, λ = 0.1; W mean of q over the host's last 32 flows; A conformal CUSUM C ← max(0, C + log g(u)), g(u) = mean over κ ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5} of κ u^(κ−1); A-V: A with the Ville threshold (alarm if C ≥ log(2/α) or u ≤ α/2, α = 0.01), reported only to show where the flow-level guarantee breaks. **Gates (original rule, host-day units), each against P and against the stronger of E/W by macro:** G1 macro host-day TPR gain ≥ +0.10; G2 Bot and DoS each gain ≥ +0.10 on the clean-history subset; G3 no family loses > 0.05; G4 realised test benign host-day alarm rate ≤ 1.5%. A fails if any gate fails against P; it is reported as "no better than simple aggregation" if it passes against P but not against E/W. **Reporting.** Attacker host and host-day counts per family; 95% bootstrap CI over hosts (2000 resamples, host-day clusters); a family with < 5 attacker hosts is reported as underpowered and its gain is not called significant. Alarm control is claimed ARL-style only (flows within a host are not exchangeable); no guaranteed-FPR claim. No parameter above is changed after the first result. | Second-opinion review amendments 1–5; host-day calibration is the step that makes the budget refer to exchangeable units (host-days) rather than flows. |

New NetFlow datasets get the §3 cleaning, the fingerprint rule and the saturation rule (dataset reported as saturated if the none-bank best member reaches ≥ 0.99 TPR@1%FPR on every family) before any model comparison; families = the Attack names. C2 strong baselines: OnePass macro host-day TPR ≥ (best of RF, XGB, MCDE on the none bank under P) − 0.02 on the same datasets; the best tree on the host bank with A is reported as an upper reference. C5 int8: macro host-day TPR loss of int8 OnePass ≤ 0.02. Per-family flow-level TPR@1%FPR and realised flow FPR reported for every dataset and member as before. Families with < 5 attacker hosts are reported as underpowered. A claim that fails is reported as failed; no parameter here changes after the first confirmatory result. | Second-opinion review: confirm the seed-0 combination on unseen seeds and datasets; use every dataset that has host keys. |

| 2026-10-25 | §5, §7, §8 (three optimisation steps; written before any of them runs and before any host-level look at nf_ton seeds 1–4) | **Step 1, host-hour budget unit.** Same streams (per source host, state reset each UTC day) but the alarm budget and detection are counted per host-hour: threshold = 1% of calibration host-hours with ≥ 1 alarm; FPR = share of test benign host-hours alarming; TPR = share of attacker host-hours (≥ 1 held-out flow in that hour) with an alarm on a held-out flow. Entry: ≥ 200 calibration host-hours. Datasets: nf_cic seeds 1–4, nf_ton seeds 1–4 (seed 0 excluded: its host-day result was seen). C1–C6 are re-evaluated in host-hour units and reported as the host-hour analysis; the host-day results stay primary for nf_cic. **Step 2, nested-LOFO tuning of the one-pass MLP** (the leak-free counterpart of ARLHNIDS's GWO + Optuna). Per outer held-out family k and seed, for each known attack family j: fit on the fit rows minus {k, j}, score j's train + val rows as pseudo-unknown (host-context population minus k, as in the bank's pair rule), threshold at 1% on the bank's cal benign rows; inner score = mean over j of flow-level TPR@1%FPR. Grid (8): trunk {plain MLP, spectral-normalised residual (DDU)} × width {64, 128} × depth {2, 3}; unknown score = 1 − p(Benign) for plain, −log q(h) for DDU. The best grid point (ties → fewer weights) is refit on the full fit rows and scored on the outer test rows as mlp_tuned (plus mlp_tuned_int8 for plain trunks). The held-out family k is never used. T1: tuned model (host variant, CUSUM A) macro host-day TPR on nf_cic ≥ OnePass + 0.03 with CI > 0; and flow-level macro TPR@1%FPR ≥ mlp_ce + 0.03 on ≥ 2 of nf_cic, nf_ton, nf_bot. **Step 3, rare-class balancing of the known families** (training rows only; test and calibration untouched). Default MLP (d–64–32–k), two arms: mlp_bal = CE with inverse-frequency class weights (mean 1); mlp_smote = SMOTE (k = 5) raising every known attack class below the fit cap (40k) to the cap, benign untouched. GAMO is not run here (a GAN per class per fold per seed is beyond the compute budget; the ARLHNIDS reproduction already compares GAMO and SMOTE). B1, per arm: flow-level macro TPR@1%FPR ≥ mlp_ce + 0.03 on ≥ 2 of the three NetFlow datasets; known-family macro-F1 on test_known reported. **Scope.** Steps 2–3 run on the host variant for nf_cic seeds 1–4, nf_ton seeds 0–4, nf_bot seeds 0–4, written as config=outer_k/tuned.parquet next to the bank parts. All results are reported whether they pass or fail. | User request: host-hour calibration, nested tuning, and an honest test of oversampling. |

| 2026-10-26 | §8 (new claim C7b; written before any back-off result on CICIoT2023 exists — its banks hold no class posteriors) | **C7b, likelihood back-off on CICIoT2023 (ciciot_csv, variant none, seeds 0–4, all outer configs incl. the DoS / DDoS / Flood+Mirai hold-outs).** Parents, fixed now from the dataset's own 7-category scheme: Volumetric {DoS, DDoS, Mirai} (bank family Flood = DoS ∪ DDoS), Access {BruteForce, Web}, Recon, Spoofing. Per outer config, fit the default MLP (d–64–32–k, CE) and the DDU model (spectral-normalised residual MLP + class Gaussians) on the bank's fit rows, as in score_bank. Flagged flows: test_unknown flows above the method's own 1% threshold on the bank's cal benign rows (DDU: −log q(h); MLP: 1 − p(Benign)). **DDU back-off** (claim): parent = argmax over parents of logsumexp over its known attack children of log π_c + log N(h; μ_c, Σ_c). **Softmax back-off** (control, the C7 rule): parent with the largest summed known-attack posterior. Leaf cases: held-out family with ≥ 1 known sibling (DoS, DDoS, Flood, Mirai, BruteForce, Web). **Primary: macro over leaf families** of the share of flagged flows given the correct parent, pooled over seeds; PASS if ≥ 0.6 and ≥ 2 × chance (chance = 1 / number of parents with a known child). Flow-weighted accuracy and the per-family confusion are reported. Result reported whatever it is; parents are not changed after it. | User request (A + B): one fresh, pre-registered back-off test. |

| 2026-10-29 | §5, §7, §8 (new claims W0–W6, WAGER; written before any WAGER code or result exists) | **Data.** nf_cic, seeds 1–4, existing LOFO folds and DDU banks (mlp_sn_gda), same hash host split, α = 0.01, horizon = one UTC day. **Relabel first (T0):** Web attacker = src 18.218.115.60 → 172.31.69.28:80 only; Infiltration attacker = src 172.31.69.24 only; all other rows labelled Web or Infiltration are dropped (unknown truth). The existing OnePass A arm is re-scored on the relabelled data as the baseline. **WAGER, fixed now (no tuning):** lane = DDU argmax class. Benign calibration flows predicted into a lane form its reference set; a lane with < 100 reference flows merges into one "attack-any" lane. Per flow, lane-wise conformal u for the known score (1 − p(Benign)) and the unknown score (−log GDA density); e = ½[g(u_k) + g(u_u)], with g the existing κ ∈ {.05, .1, .2, .5} power mixture. Internal hosts: C[h,ℓ] ← max(0, C[h,ℓ] + log e). Host statistic M_h(t) = running max over the day of max_ℓ C[h,ℓ]. p̂ = (1 + #{calibration host-day M ≥ M_h(t)})/(n+1). Unbounded keys (dst:port, src /24): count-min, d = 4, w = 2048, blake2b keyed hash with a secret salt, Z ← Z·e^(−Δt/600 s) + [log e]₊, calibrated the same way on benign key-days built from calibration hosts only. Alarm: p̂ ≤ α. Throttle factor α/p̂. Block: p̂ = 1/(n+1). **Pass rules.** **W1** (no manipulation): WAGER macro host-day TPR ≥ OnePass A − 0.02 on the relabelled data, mean over seeds. **W2** (collateral): for k ∈ {1, 2, 10}, the pooled share of test benign host-days with min p̂ ≤ α/k, and the share blocked, are not significantly above α/k + 1/(n+1) and 1/(n+1) (one-sided exact binomial, 0.05), per seed. **W0** (damage bound): per family, D = mean log e over attack-lane flows of attacker host-days, B = max log e, c* = max calibration M. Pass if for every family with D > 0, mean over attacker host-days of min(τ, attack flows that day) ≤ (c* + B)/D, where τ = attack-lane flows until block. **W3** (padding): insert r ∈ {1, 4, 16, 64} benign flows per attack flow into each attacker host's stream (sampled from same-day test benign hosts, interleaved in time, host-context features recomputed with HostFlows). Pass if WAGER macro TPR at r = 16 ≥ 0.8 × its r = 0 value. The padding-defence claim is made only if OnePass A at r = 16 is also < 0.8 × its own r = 0; otherwise it is reported as "padding not a threat here" and dropped. **W4** (Sybil): split each attacker's flows round-robin over k ∈ {1, 4, 16, 64} sources in its /24. Detection = any alarm on a key carrying its flows. Pass if WAGER at k = 16 ≥ 0.8 × its k = 1 TPR, with benign key-day alarm share not significantly above α (as W2). OnePass A (src only) is reported alongside. **W5** (spoofing): add 10⁵ external sources, each with 1–3 flows resampled from benign rows. Pass if sketch memory is unchanged in bytes, internal-host macro TPR changes by ≤ 0.01, and the benign key alarm share passes the W2 test. **W6** (adaptive attacker): cross-entropy policy search (4 seeds, 50 iterations × 32 episodes; one episode = one attacker day) over padding r, Sybil k, pacing, and mimicry quantile q (send only the family's attack flows whose score is below its q-quantile). Reward = delivered attack flows, scaled by the throttle factor in force. Pass if, for every family, mean delivered flows of the final policy ≤ (c* + B)/D_π, where D_π is measured on the policy's own sent attack flows (no bound is claimed where D_π ≤ 0). **Stopping.** Order: T0, W1, W2, W0, W3, W4, W5, W6. Stop if W1 or W3 fails. No changes to κ, d, w, decay, lane merge rule, r, k, or seeds after any result; additions are labelled exploratory. | New method; pass rules fixed before any code. |

Keys: exact per-(source, day, lane) CUSUM as in W1, plus dst:port and src /24 count-min sketches (d = 4, w = 2048, keyed blake2b, Z ← Z·e^(−Δt/600 s) + [log e]₊, reset each UTC day; a key's value at a flow = min over rows after adding it); each key type is calibrated on key-day maxima of the cal-role rows of benign calibration hosts; alarm p̂ ≤ α per key type; an attacker host-day is detected when any of its keys alarms at one of its held-out flows. | 2026-10-29 | §5, §7, §8 (new claim CAL, few-host calibration; written before any code or result) | **Question.** Can the per-host CUSUM alarm threshold hold a 1% benign host-day budget when only a few benign hosts are available to calibrate? **Data.** nf_cic and nf_ton, host variant, seeds 1–4, all outer folds, α = 0.01, host-day unit; nf_cic attacker rows relabelled (T0 mask, `data/processed/nf_cic_relabel.npz`). Score: DDU unknown score `mlp_sn_gda`; increment X = log g(u), g the existing κ ∈ {.05, .1, .2, .5} mixture, u the conformal p-value against the rank rows of the m calibration hosts; CUSUM per host-day as in `host_stream.stats`. **Calibration subsets:** m ∈ {5, 20, 50} hosts, 10 seeded draws each, from benign calibration hosts with ≥ 1 cal-role host-day. **Methods** (same streams): **M** (new) — X binned into 8 pooled quantile bins (x_b = bin mean), first-order transition matrix P over consecutive flows within host-days of the m hosts' cal rows (+1 smoothing), θ* > 0 with spectral radius ρ(P·diag(e^{θ x_b})) = 1 (bisection; no root → "not calibratable", counted as alarming every host-day), K = max h / min h (Perron right eigenvector at θ*), alarm when C_t > [log(K/α) + log(t(t+1))]/θ*, t = flow index in the host-day; **E** — `host_stream.threshold` on the m hosts' host-day maxima; **V** — the existing Ville rule AV; **B** — moving-block bootstrap, 2,000 synthetic host-days from blocks of 32 of the m hosts' X sequences with lengths drawn from their host-day lengths, threshold = 99% quantile of CUSUM maxima; **Full** — E with all calibration hosts (TPR reference). Benign test host-days: benign non-calibration hosts; detection credit: alarm at one of the attacker host-day's held-out flows. **Pass (M):** at m = 5 and m = 20, realised benign test host-day alarm rate ≤ 0.015 on both datasets (mean over draws and seeds) and nf_cic macro host-day TPR ≥ Full − 0.05. **Novelty condition:** a new-method claim only if B fails the same rate rule at m = 5 or 20, or both pass and M's nf_cic TPR ≥ B's; otherwise reported as "block bootstrap suffices". nf_ton TPR reported without a rule. **Stopping:** M failing the primary rule drops the calibration direction (reported as a failure). No change to bins, block length, draws, m or seeds after results; additions are exploratory. | New method; rules fixed before code. |

| 2026-10-29 | §9 DISC (implementation and deviations; before any loader output or score for CTU-13 / IoT-23) | (1) **CIC-2018:** the existing nf_cic host banks (seeds 1–4; same CIC-2018 traffic, NetFlow-v3 view) with the T0 relabel replace the Engelen improved CSVs. IN_BYTES = SrcBytes, OUT_BYTES = TotBytes − SrcBytes, IN_PKTS = TotPkts, OUT_PKTS = 1 if OUT_BYTES > 0 else 0 (packet split not in Argus output). (3) **IoT-23:** first 300,000 rows of each capture (time prefix; RAM); host key = capture:IP; Malicious rows take the capture's malware family (IoT-23 README: 1 HideAndSeek, 3 Muhstik, 7/34/35/43/44/48/49/52 Mirai, 8 Hakai, 9 Hajime, 17/33 Kenjiro, 20/21 Torii, 36 Okiru, 39 IRCBot, 42 Trojan, 60 Gagfyt); benign hosts = sources with no malicious flow. (4) **Detection:** each detector is scored with P (host-day max of the flow score) and A (per-host conformal CUSUM), both at the 1% host-day budget from `host_stream`. "Missed by every detector" = no alarm from any (detector, method) pair. Seeds 1–4. (5) **B2 CV:** direction chosen on the training folds, AUC pooled over the test folds. Implementation changes before scoring (no effect on earlier banks): FeatureGDA uses the jitter-only covariance for a class with one row; the bank skips empty roles and drops early-stop rows of classes absent from the fit; NIDS_LEAN=1 skips the KD/tail/MCDE extras that DISC does not use. | 2026-10-29 | §9 ADV (adaptive attacker; written before any perturbed bank or result) | **Adaptive-attacker characterization.** Threat model: the attacker knows the detector design (features, 10 s / 120 s host windows, CUSUM) but not the trained weights or thresholds. It can change the timing of its own flows and add benign-looking cover flows from the attacking host. It cannot remove attack flows or alter their per-flow content. **Perturbations apply only to the test-time host-context population;** fit, early-stop, bsample, rank and cal roles use clean traffic. (a) **Dilation** k ∈ {1, 10, 100, 1000}: per (source host, family), start times t → t0 + k(t − t0), where t0 = that group's first flow; flow order and per-flow fields unchanged. (b) **Cover traffic** r ∈ {1, 2, 4, 8}: for each (attacker host, family) group, r × (its flow count) copies of benign flows sampled (seeded) from benign hosts, source IP set to the attacker, start times uniform over the group's span; cover flows are scored by every detector and feed the attacker's host streams, and an alarm on a cover flow counts for that attacker host-day. k = 1 must reproduce the DISC scores exactly. **Measured** per (dataset, detector, method P/A): macro host TPR (any alarm across the attack), macro host-day TPR, realised benign host-day FPR; attack completion-time ratio = k. **Claim rule:** "robust to pacing up to k×" (or "to cover ratio r") only where macro host TPR ≥ 0.5 × its unperturbed value on ≥ 2 of 3 datasets; otherwise it is reported as an evasion. Datasets nf_cic (T0 relabel), nf_ctu13, nf_iot23; detectors mlp_sn_gda, rf, xgb, iforest, kitnet; seeds 1–4; same 1% budget and calibration hosts as DISC. Denial-of-state (spoofed-source flood) is measured on the streaming engine and pre-registered with it. | Rules fixed before data. |
| 2026-10-29 | §9 ADV (implementation and deviations; before any perturbed score) | (1) Cover traffic per (attacker host, family) group is r × min(n, 20,000) flows, so groups over 20,000 flows (floods) get a lower cover ratio than r (RAM: nf_cic and IoT-23 hold 2.6M and 3.5M attack flows). (2) Cover start times are uniform over [first − 60 s, last + 60 s] of the group, so one-flow groups also get cover. (3) The bank scores a capped sample of each held-out family's test-split flows, so scored cover flows are thinned to each attacker's scored share of its own flows. The cover:attack ratio inside the scored streams therefore stays ≈ r. (4) Cover flows copy every per-flow field of a random benign flow from a benign host (seeded); only source IP and start time change. (5) Host TPR = share of attacker hosts (per held-out family, macro) with ≥ 1 alarmed host-day. (6) A run order is fixed: CTU-13 and IoT-23 first (parallel, cores 0–5 / 6–11), nf_cic last (alone, RAM). | Fixed before results. |
| 2026-10-29 | §9 FWD (UGR'16 forward-time test; written before any UGR'16 loader output, label count or score) | **Question:** does the 1% benign host-day budget, calibrated on one period, hold weeks later on the same real network? **Data:** UGR'16, the Spanish-ISP NetFlow v9 trace (HF parquet mirror `Lystea/UGR16-PARQUET`). Weeks: June 3 and June 4 (calibration set, no injected attacks), July 5 and August 1–2 (test set, attacks 28 Jul – 9 Aug). **Rows:** seeded hash sample of 0.1% of source IPs; plus every flow of the 5 attacker machines and 25 victims (UGR'16 paper Table V); plus every flow of a seeded 5% sample of source IPs that carry a real-anomaly label. `blacklist` rows dropped; `background` = benign (screened by the authors, not verified; stated as a caveat). NetFlow is unidirectional: IN_* = the record's packets / bytes, OUT_BYTES = 0, OUT_PKTS = 1 if the TCP ACK flag is set or the protocol is not TCP (a reply indicator, so `fail` keeps its meaning for SYN-only scans). Host key = IP. **Forward split:** fit, early-stop, rank and cal benign rows come from June only; known-attack training rows come from July week 5; test = August weeks 1–2 (benign and every family). July benign rows are unused (keeps a ≥ 4-week gap between calibration and test). Families = `label_attack` values with ≥ 5 attacker hosts or the 5 synthetic families, held out one at a time (LOFO); a family must have rows in both July week 5 and August to be scored. Detectors, methods (P, A), 1% budget and hash-split calibration hosts as DISC. Seeds 1–4. **Claim rule:** "the 1% budget holds under forward-time shift" for a (detector, method) only if the 95% host-bootstrap CI upper bound of its realised benign host-day FPR on August test hosts is ≤ 1.5%. Otherwise we report the inflation factor (realised / 1%). **Decomposition (reported, no claim rule):** time-only FPR = August benign host-days of the calibration hosts; host-only FPR = June held-out-role benign host-days of non-calibration hosts. **Cross-dataset:** models fit on nf_ctu13 with every family scored on UGR'16 August nerisbotnet host-days, thresholded on UGR'16 June calibration hosts; host-day TPR reported. | Rules fixed before data. |
| 2026-10-29 | §9 ENG (engine scale and denial-of-state; written after the parity run, before any scale or flood run) | **Scale (characterization):** the C engine replays LANL 2015 flows (`new_dataset/flows.txt.gz`, 130M flows, 36.2 days, enterprise network). Fields map to the engine record as IN_PKTS = packets, IN_BYTES = bytes, OUT_BYTES = 0, OUT_PKTS = 1 (no reply split); the model is the parity model (nf_iot23 s1, held Okiru), so LANL scores have no detection meaning and only memory, latency and eviction counts are reported. Host-table sizes 1,024 / 8,192 / 65,536 / 262,144 slots; measured: resident memory (max RSS), µs/flow, evictions, distinct source hosts. **Denial-of-state (characterization + one claim rule):** the IoT-23 parity stream (captures c7, c34, ch4) with N spoofed one-flow sources injected per real flow, N ∈ {0, 1, 10, 100}; each spoofed flow copies the per-flow fields of a random benign flow of the stream, has a fresh random source key, the real flow's time, and a random benign destination (seeded). Table sizes 512 / 4,096 / 65,536. Also crossed with ADV dilation k ∈ {1, 10, 100} of the Mirai hosts' flow times. Measured: share of attacker host-days with an A alarm, share with a P alarm, benign host-day A/P alarm rates, first-alarm delay (flows of the attacker host until the first A alarm), evictions of attacker-host entries. **Claim rule:** "state survives a spoofing flood at table size T" only if every attacker host-day that alarms at N = 0 also alarms at N = 100 for that (T, k). Otherwise it is reported as a denial-of-state evasion with the smallest failing (N, k). Only 3 attacker host-days exist in this stream, so the result is stated as an existence result, not a rate. | Rules fixed before any scale or flood run. |
| 2026-10-29 | §9 ENG result (flood) | Claim rule holds: every attacker host-day alarming at N = 0 alarms at N = 100 for all T ∈ {512, 4096, 65536}, k ∈ {1, 10, 100} (3 / 10 / 64 attacker host-days; existence result; first-alarm delay grows to max 40 flows). Unplanned, not under a claim rule: spoofed flows induce benign false alarms through destination-side context: A-alarmed benign host-days 1.4% → 99.1% at k = 1, N = 10 (215 host-days). Details: results/engine/flood.md. | Reported as measured. |

Fails for 4: DDU (mlp_sn_gda) A 1.44% [1.10, 1.80] (1.4×), DDU P 1.29% [1.03, 1.55] (1.3×), rf A 1.49% [1.10, 1.92] (1.5×), xgb A 1.23% [0.89, 1.61] (1.2×). Inflation is at most 1.5×, far below the 5–8× seen on CTU-13/IoT-23 (86/105 test host-days). Details: results/engine/lanl_scale.md. | Reported as measured. |

| 2026-10-29 | §9 TOK (FlowTok prototype kill test; written before any FlowTok code ran) | **Model:** per-flow tokenizer → per-host sequence coder → existing conformal P / A layer. Tokenizer FT-tree = XGB 50 rounds, depth 4, on per-flow features only (`bank.feats`, no hc_*); token = (argmax known class, confidence bucket {< 0.6, 0.6–0.9, ≥ 0.9}, rarity bit). Rarity = mean over trees of −log(training share of the flow's leaf); bit = rarity above the 99th percentile of fit rows. Coder: per known family, interpolated (Witten–Bell) variable-order Markov model, orders 0–3, over each source host's token stream in time order (population = kept rows minus the role's removal set, as `Bank.frame`); per-flow score = −log2 P_benign(token \| previous ≤ 3 tokens of the host); known label = family with the shortest code over the host's last 32 tokens. **Arms:** ft_tree (primary), ft_order0 (same tokens, order 0), ft_mlp (int8 MLP tokenizer on per-flow features, no rarity bit), ft_hc (FT-tree tokenizer with hc_* added). **Baselines:** rf, xgb, kitnet, mlp_sn_gda from the base bank of the same seed and config (rows asserted identical). **Metric:** macro over held-out families of host-day TPR (`host_stream.fold`, 1% budget); per detector the better of P / A. UGR'16 scores only families with July week 5 and August rows (§9 FWD rule). **Gate (seed 1; nf_ugr16 forward, nf_ctu13, nf_iot23):** FlowTok continues only if ft_tree TPR ≥ (max baseline TPR − 0.05) on ≥ 2 of 3 datasets AND on nf_ugr16 its forward benign FPR 95% host-bootstrap CI upper ≤ 1.5% (for the method used); on nf_ctu13 / nf_iot23 (≈ 100 benign host-days) its FPR must be ≤ the max baseline FPR of the same method. Otherwise FlowTok is killed and reported. ft_mlp / ft_order0 / ft_hc are reported, never used to pass the gate. Full claim rules (seeds 1–4, + nf_cic, flood, ADV) as written in the Phase 3 plan, to be fixed in a later row before those runs. | Rules fixed before any FlowTok run. |

Reported arms: ft_order0 0.54 / 0.70 / 0.57, ft_mlp 0.44 / 0.67 / 0.89, ft_hc 0.62 / 0.56 / 0.83 (UGR / CTU / IoT, A). Reading: dropping host context (the spoofing surface) costs 0.2–0.4 TPR against full RF/XGB, and the sequence coder does not recover it (ft_tree vs ft_order0: −0.04 / 0.00 / +0.04). Host context from the role's population as `Bank.frame`. **Arms (reported, not used for the gate):** ple_ce (plain MLP on the same encoding, 1 − P(benign)); clip_ddu (bank Prep features clipped to the fit-row [0.1%, 99.9%] quantiles, then SN-MLP + GDA). **Gate:** identical to §9 TOK with ple_ddu in place of ft_tree (seed 1; nf_ugr16 forward, nf_ctu13, nf_iot23; TPR ≥ max baseline − 0.05 on ≥ 2 of 3 with the FPR conditions of §9 TOK). **Flood (reported with the gate, no separate claim):** ple_ddu and clip_ddu added to `flood_detectors.py` (§9 ENG-X streams); the ENG-X claim rule is applied to them. If the gate fails, the model is dropped and reported. | Rules fixed before any PLE run. |

Reported arms: ple_ce 0.69 / 0.71 / 0.72, clip_ddu 0.43 / 0.70 / 0.56 (UGR / CTU / IoT); ple_ce also fails the gate rule (UGR: A 0.694 has CI upper 1.84%, P 0.660 < 0.665; IoT 0.72 vs 1.00). **Flood (ENG-X streams, k = 1, N = 10, benign host-days alarmed A / P):** ple_ddu 0.5% / 0.5%, ple_ce 0.0% / 0.5% (not affected under the ENG-X rule), clip_ddu 0.5% / 98.1% (affected), vs mlp_sn_gda 0.5% / 98.1%. | 2026-10-29 | §9 SEL (referee fix + dataset set + development selection; written before any candidate run) | **Why:** IoT-23 has 1–3 attacker host-days per held-out family (~14 total), so host-day TPR differences of one host-day decided the §9 TOK / PLE / TABM gates there; CTU-13, ToN-IoT, Bot-IoT are also host-level underpowered (≤ 27 attacker host-days per family). **Datasets (fixed by availability of IPs, timestamps and host score banks, not by results):** nf_ugr16 (forward), nf_cic, nf_ctu13, nf_iot23, nf_ton, nf_bot. **Metrics:** (1) primary: flow TPR at a matched 1% benign flow FPR — threshold = 99th percentile of the detector's score on cal-role benign flows of calibration hosts; TPR = share of test_unknown flows above it, macro over held-out families; flow FPR on test_benign flows of non-calibration hosts reported; (2) host-day TPR (`host_stream.fold`, better of P / A), deciding only for powered families (≥ 10 attacker host-days in that seed's test split), others labelled underpowered; (3) nf_ugr16 forward benign FPR (§9 FWD). Differences get 95% bootstrap CIs resampling attacker hosts (flows clustered by host): beats = CI lower > 0, non-inferior = CI lower ≥ −0.05. **Candidates (fixed):** tabm, tabm_min, tabm_raw (§9 TABM), ple_ce (§9 PLE), lxgb_hc (XGB 50 rounds depth 4 on per-flow + hc_*). **Baselines:** rf, xgb, mlp_sn_gda from the base bank; kitnet from the base bank where present (nf_ugr16, nf_ctu13, nf_iot23), else refit in the candidate run exactly as `score_bank.run_config` (RobustPrep + KitNET on the bank's benign sample). **Selection (seed 1 = development):** among candidates not affected by the ENG-X flood rule (k = 1, N = 10), the one with the highest mean over the 6 datasets of (candidate flow TPR − best baseline flow TPR); its host-day method (P or A) = the better seed-1 mean. Disclosed: tabm / ple seed-1 results on 3 datasets were seen before this row. Confirmation rules (seeds 2–4) go in §9 CONF before those runs. | Rules fixed before any candidate run. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 SEL amendment (metric defect; before nf_cic seed 1 exists and before any confirmation run) | The registered primary metric was "flow TPR at a matched 1% benign flow FPR" with the threshold set on calibration hosts. On the 5 dev datasets the realised test-host flow FPR of that threshold was not 1% and differed by detector (nf_ugr16 26–29%, nf_bot 5–15%, nf_ctu13 2–5%), so detectors were compared at unequal FPR, against the registered intent. Fix: the threshold is the 99th percentile of each detector's score on test_benign flows of non-calibration benign hosts (ROC TPR at 1% FPR, equal for every detector); the calibration-host threshold's realised FPR / TPR are reported as "deployable" only. Selection rule, candidates, datasets, CIs and margins unchanged. Disclosed: the 5-dataset dev table under the defective metric was seen before this amendment (tabm_min provisionally selected; all candidates below best baseline). Also disclosed: in nf_bot 3 of 4 held-out families are run by the same 8 attacker hosts as known families and benign is 1% of flows; nf_bot stays in the registered set and is flagged in the report. | Amendment before nf_cic and confirmation. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 SEL amendment 2 (data defects found by `scripts/dataset_audit.py`; before any rerun under the fixed scores, before nf_cic seed 1 and any confirmation) | (1) **Score representation:** candidate scores were stored as float32 1 − P(benign), which saturates at exactly 1.0 for P < 6e-8 (nf_ugr16 dos: 2–4% of test-benign flows tied at 1.0 with up to 47% of unknown flows). Candidates now output −log P(benign) (same ranking, no saturation); all candidate parts are recomputed. Baseline ties at the threshold are ≤ 0.8% (audit) and baselines are not recomputed. (2) **Ties:** the flow metric is the randomised-threshold ROC point at exactly 1% FPR (tied flows get the fractional weight). (3) **Eligibility, data-only (no detector output used):** test_unknown flows count only if their source host runs exactly one attack family in the whole dataset (otherwise the "unknown" attacker was in training under another family); a (dataset, family) decides only with ≥ 100 such flows; a dataset decides only with ≥ 1000 test-benign flows from ≥ 20 non-attacker non-calibration hosts. Seed-1 audit result: deciding = nf_ugr16 (nerisbotnet, spam, udpscan), nf_cic (Bot, BruteForce, DDoS, DoS, Infiltration), nf_ctu13 (Menti, Murlo, NSIS, Neris, Rbot, Virut); excluded: nf_iot23 (455 test-benign flows), nf_ton (37 unknown flows from single-family attackers), nf_bot (0, and 209 test-benign flows from 2 hosts) — reported in an appendix with these reasons. Selection = highest mean gap over deciding datasets; rule otherwise unchanged. The §9 CONF dataset-count criterion will be set against the 3 deciding datasets. Disclosed: dev tables under the defective metric were seen. | Amendment before any rerun. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 CARD — 10-category scorecard, ours (the §9 SEL selected model) vs the heavy models RF and XGB (written before any result under the amended metric exists) | **Win** in a category = ours better than **both** RF and XGB by the rule below; **tie** = not worse than either by more than the margin; **loss** otherwise. Accuracy categories use the 3 deciding datasets (§9 SEL amendment 2), seeds 2–4 pooled, host-cluster bootstrap; win = CI lower bound of the difference > 0 vs each of RF and XGB on ≥ 2 of 3 datasets, tie = CI lower ≥ −0.05 on ≥ 2 of 3. (1) **Unknown attacks, flow:** TPR at matched 1% benign flow FPR (fresh-attacker flows). (2) **Unknown attacks, host:** host-day TPR, pre-fixed P/A method, powered families only. (3) **Known attacks:** test_known flow TPR at matched 1% FPR. (4) **Forward-time false alarms:** nf_ugr16 realised August benign host-day FPR at the deployable (calibration-host) threshold; win = upper CI below both RF and XGB point values and ≤ 1.5%. (5) **Deployment gap:** realised test-host flow FPR at the calibration-host threshold, |realised − 1%| averaged over deciding datasets; win = smaller than both by ≥ 25% relative. (6) **Spoof flood:** §9 ENG-X stream, benign host-day alarm rate at k ∈ {1, 10}, N = 10; win = lower than both in all 4 (k, method) cells, tie = within 1 percentage point. (7) **Adaptive evasion:** the §9 ADV attack grid (dilate / cover) run against ours, RF and XGB on nf_cic and nf_ugr16; win = higher mean TPR under attack with CI lower > 0 vs both. (8) **Model size:** int8 serialized bytes; win = ≤ 1/10 of the smaller heavy model. (9) **Latency:** median per-flow inference on the Pi (single core, batch 1); win = ≤ 1/2 of both. (10) **Runtime memory:** peak RSS of the scorer on the Pi; win = ≤ 1/2 of both. Reported as wins / ties / losses with every number; target stated in advance by the author: ≥ 7 wins. Categories 8–10 are efficiency and are reported separately from 1–7 as well, so a reader can see the accuracy-only count. | Scorecard fixed before results. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 SEL amendment 3 (candidate added before any result under the amended metric exists) | Adds candidates **tabm_kd** and **tabm_kd_min**: TabM-mini (as §9 TABM) on target-aware bin edges (per feature, the thresholds of a 16-leaf decision tree on that feature alone, Gorishniy et al. NeurIPS'22 PLE-T), trained by member-averaged KD (KL at T = 4, as `models.train_mlp`) to 3-fold out-of-fold XGB (300 × depth 8) probabilities on the fit rows; scores −log mean / min member P(benign). Disclosed: a plain MLP distilled from OOF XGB (`mlp_kd`) existed in Phase 1 banks. Selection rule unchanged; nf_ctu13 and nf_ugr16 candidate parts are recomputed with the full candidate set. | Added before results. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 BOWL (evolutionary tournament; written before any bowl run) | **Arena (training side only):** per deciding dataset (nf_ctu13, nf_ugr16, nf_cic), seed 1, up to 6 nested pairs (R0, j) (`scripts/arena.py`): fit = `bank.fit_rows(R0 ∪ {j})`; pseudo-unknown = j's capped train + validation rows from single-family attacker hosts (≥ 100), host context with only R0 removed; benign = cal rows of benign calibration hosts; known = es attack rows; asserted disjoint from every test role. **Fitness:** mean of pseudo-unknown TPR and known-attack TPR at exactly 1% benign FPR (`roc.roc_detect`), macro over pairs. **Limits (every fighter):** size ≤ 64 KB (int8 parameters + float32 bin edges + tree nodes × 8 B); speed: single-thread wall-clock per row at batch 4096 ≤ 0.5 × heavy XGB (300 × depth 8) measured in the same process (op-count formulas were dropped because tree visits and SIMD MACs are not comparable). **Fighters:** neural or neural hybrids only (MLP / TabM with raw, quantile-bin, tree-bin or periodic encoders; NODE-lite; GBDT-leaf → net); genes: encoder, width, depth, members, spectral norm, activation, unknown head {−log P(benign), min-member, energy, latent Mahalanobis}, KD weight to 3-fold OOF XGB, label smoothing, focal loss, lr. Bosses (heavy RF, XGB) are reference only. **Round:** NSGA-II (Optuna 5.0), population 32 × 8 generations, cheap fitness (3 pairs / dataset, 40k fit rows, 15 epochs); death match of the feasible Pareto front (≤ 10) on all pairs, full rows, 2 seeds, 30 epochs; champion = best death-match fitness; autopsy vs boss XGB; next roster = crossovers + power-ups chosen from the autopsy, each power-up kept only if it beats the 2-seed noise on the arena; feature-side power-ups are also given to the bosses. **Stop:** champion fitness ≥ both bosses' on the full arena, or 5 rounds. Round 1 runs on the datasets whose arena exists at its start (nf_cic is added when built); disclosed. **Hand-off:** the final champion is "ours" for §9 CARD; it must pass the §9 ENG-X flood rule (else the next feasible neural genome); confirmation is single-shot on seeds 2–4 test rows of the 3 deciding datasets under §9 CONF, written before that run. | 2026-10-30 | §9 CERT: spoof-certified detection (written before any CERT code or result exists) | **Threat model:** an attacker injects flows with arbitrary fields (spoofed source, any destination, port, OUT_PKTS, IN_BYTES) but cannot delete flows; budget m = injected flows per host key (source or destination) in any 240 s interval (2 × the largest host-context window, because an injected flow before a window can remove the novelty of one real flow inside it). Calibration data is clean (disclosed). **Theorem 1 (exact per-flow sensitivity of hostctx.HostFlows):** for a flow whose window (key k, width w) holds n real flows, c of them novel (newport / newpeer) or failed, byte sum S, any j ≤ m injections keep: n in [n, n+m]; fail share in [c/(n+m), (c+m)/(n+m)]; newport/newpeer share in [(c−m)⁺/(n+m), (c+m)/(n+m)]; mean bytes ≥ S/(n+m), unbounded above (bounded only by the cert-mode input clip of host-context columns to the fit range [q0.001, q0.999]). Mapped through Prep (monotone per feature) this is a per-row box [lo, hi] (`scripts/sensitivity.py`); soundness is brute-force tested by injecting random flows through HostFlows. **Theorem 2 (certificate):** interval bound propagation of the box through a network with monotone encoder (raw + clip, quantile or tree bins; periodic excluded) and monotone activations gives a sound upper bound s⁺ on s = −log P(benign) (last affine layer on z_i − z_benign; ensembles by member bounds). Certified FPR(m) = share of benign rows (host-days: any flow) with s⁺ ≥ τ; no m-flow injection can push the benign alarm rate above it. The same box certificate is computed for RF / XGB (per-tree reachable-leaf bounds; XGB margins summed, RF P(benign) averaged). **Certified training (bowl power-up cert):** CE + λ · CE on IBP worst-case logits of benign fit rows at m_train = 10; λ ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5, 1}; round-4 constraint certified FPR(10) on arena cal rows ≤ 2%. **Kill tests:** **K1** (threat real on deciding data, run first): a realisable adaptive spoof (m ≤ 50, search over count, novelty, fail and bytes patterns; `scripts/spoof_attack.py`) raises the benign host-day alarm rate of at least one of RF, XGB or the round-3 champion to ≥ 10% and ≥ 5 × clean on ≥ 2 of 3 of CTU-13 / UGR'16 / CIC (seed 1, one held-out config each); fail → CERT direction stops and is reported. **K2:** best certified genome full-arena fitness ≥ 0.98 × best uncertified champion and certified flow FPR(10) ≤ 2% on arena cal rows; fail → certificate stays an analysis tool only. **K3** (reported either way): certified FPR(m), m ∈ {1, 5, 10, 50, 100}, ours vs box-certified RF / XGB. **K4** (soundness): attacked empirical FPR ≤ certified FPR on every config; a violation is a bug, fixed before any claim. **Hand-off:** the §9 CARD "ours" becomes the best bowl genome satisfying K2 (else the uncertified champion, disclosed); §9 CONF adds the certified FPR curve and the adaptive spoof attack on the champion. | Fixed before any CERT result. |
| 2026-10-30 | §9 CERT K1 execution details (before any K1 run) | `scripts/spoof_attack.py`: outer config held = first family of the dataset, seed 1; 300 benign test host-days (seed 0); host context recomputed exactly on every flow sharing a key with a victim plus the injected flows (harness check: clean recomputation must equal the bank's host context, else no result); the attacker knows victim flow times and injects greedily right before each victim flow, never exceeding m per targeted key in any 240 s interval; grid side {dst, src, both} × new peer {0, 1} × new port {0, 1} × failed {0, 1} × IN_BYTES {0, 1e3, 1e9}, m ∈ {10, 50}. Detectors: RF, XGB (arena configs), latest bowl champion (round 3 if finished at run time). **Decision on host-day method P** (the §9 SEL method); A reported. | Fixed before K1 runs. |

PGD inside the box on pair_0 (λ 4): certified 13.1% vs PGD-attackable 3.9% vs clean 1.0% at m = 10. **Opening the box one group at a time (m = 10): destination-side injection alone 1.1% / 1.1% (clean level); source-side alone 44% / 24%, all of it in the 120 s window.** Freezing the bytes columns and prior-shrunk shares c / (n + n0), n0 ∈ {10, 30}, did not help (27–36%); neither is adopted. Tree box bounds (K3, in progress) are near-vacuous (RF CTU pair_0: 93% at m = 1). **Round-4 settings:** cert λ ∈ {0.5, 1, 2, 4} (replaces {0, 0.25, 0.5, 1}: λ = 0 always fails the constraint) with linear warm-up over the first half of training; encoders raw / quantile / tree; heads nlog / min; host-context clip on. Death match reports the full certificate and the destination-side-only certificate (`dst` keys). If no fighter meets certified FPR(10) ≤ 2%, the death match takes the 10 least-violating size/speed-feasible fighters (disclosed). **K2 is unchanged.** | Fixed before round 4. |

Brute-force test passes; three mutations (drop the +m novelty term, tighten the lower share, tighten n) are each caught. | 2026-10-31 | §9 CERT **K5: is the gated-tree comparison fair?** (written before any K5 code or result; `scripts/k5_trees.py`) | **Why:** our gated net is bounded by IBP after training for that bound (cert + rdet), while the gated trees used a per-tree bound (loose, trees bounded independently) and no robust training. Either could make the +0.15 gap an artefact. **Two fixes, both given to the trees:** (a) **exact verifier**: Kantchelian-style MILP (scipy / HiGHS) over the deletion box at m = 10, one binary per (varying feature, threshold), leaf variables with path constraints; RF and binary XGB: max P(benign) is exact; multiclass XGB: min over the box of max_c (z_c − z_bi), a sound lower bound log1p(exp(·)) on the score. Per row: block if the proven lower bound > tau; not block if a point realised from the MILP solution, scored by the real model, is ≤ tau; **otherwise (time limit, undecided) the row counts as blocked**, which favours the trees. MILP only on rows where the plain alarm fires and the per-tree bound does not already block. (b) **robust-trained trees** (the analogue of cert + rdet): RF / XGB refit on the fit rows plus 2 uniform samples per row from its box at m = 10 (benign rows: Theorem 1 injection box, label benign; attack rows: Theorem 1' deletion box, same label), boxes clipped to the fit-row range per column. Each tree model gets its own clean 1%-FPR tau on cal rows, as in the gated-bosses row. **Protocol:** full arena (18 pairs, bytes context zeroed), seed 1, 200 sampled rows per pair (100 unknown pseudo rows, 100 known attack rows; RNG seed 0); robust fitness at m = 10 = (unknown + known robust TPR) / 2; per-tree and MILP verifiers both reported; MILP time limit 20 s per row; undecided counts reported. **Decision rule:** the best of {RF, XGB} × {plain, robust-trained} under the MILP verifier vs our round-5 champion's 0.769: ≥ 0.719 (gap < 0.05) → the claim "gating + certified training beats gated trees by a wide margin" is dropped and reported as dropped; ≥ 0.769 → trees match or beat us, reported. < 0.719 → the comparison stands. **Soundness check:** for 20 rows per pair, the MILP bound must be ≥ the per-tree lower bound, and ≤ the score at every one of 200 random points in the box (asserted). | Fixed before K5 runs. |

| 2026-10-31 | §9 **ENGINE state exhaustion (K11)** (written before any code change or result; prompted by a review comment that an attacker can probe and escape a single IDS / IPS node) | **Gap found by analysis:** the deployed engine (`engine/certids.c`, bounded host table, LRU eviction) differs from the exact features of every Python result: injected flows from many spoofed keys (each within ≤ m per key per 240 s, inside the threat model) can evict a real host's entry, which deletes real history; Theorem 1' only covers deletion of injected flows, so a certified block on a re-created entry is not covered. **Fix:** a flow may be blocked only when both its source and destination host entries have been tracked continuously for ≥ 130 s (the longest bucketed window span); younger entries can alarm but never block (abstain). An entry that survived without eviction has complete bucketed history, so Theorem 1' applies to the engine's own features (argument in the certids.c header). **K11 test (`scripts/engine_k11.py`):** replay benign victim flows through certids with a small host table (64 slots) clean and under a spoofed-key flood that forces evictions (m flows per spoofed key, many keys, the K1 patterns on the victims' keys plus random keys); pass = zero victim flows blocked under the flood whose clean replay (same table size, no flood) does not alarm (the engine-level K4-gate), and the count of abstentions (alarm but entry too young) is reported. The same test without the age rule is reported to show whether the gap is reachable. | Fixed before any code or result. |
