Attack budget M in {10, 50, 100} injected flows per key per 240 s (certified box stays m = 10). Patterns: the 24 distinct K4 patterns (side x peer x port x fail; the nbytes axis is dropped because host-context byte columns are zeroed before every detector, so its 3 values give identical inputs) plus 8 random mixtures per M (per-flow peer / port / fail chosen with per-pattern probabilities ~ U(0,1), RNG 0). Adaptive attacker: per victim flow, a block under any pattern counts (OR over patterns). Rules: M = 10, any violation (block under attack without a clean alarm) for B = bug, fix before any claim (soundness). M = 50, 100: violations and block_day reported as measured for all three detectors (graceful degradation; no pass / fail). | Fixed before any run. |
| 2026-11-01 | §9 **K18 adversarial-training baseline (written before any run)** | Competitor = the only prior defence class for injection (empirical adversarial training, e.g. arXiv 2608.24454; Madry PGD). AT = B's genome with cert = 0, rdet = 0 and instead PGD adversarial training in the same boxes and weights: adv = 4 (10-step PGD, random start, step 2.5 x box width / 10, maximising -log P(benign) on the m = 10 insertion box of benign fit rows; loss = mean-member CE to Benign at the PGD point) and advd = 2 (same PGD on the m = 10 deletion box of attack fit rows, maximising log P(benign); loss = -log(1 - P(benign))). Same seed, data, epochs, KD, tau_day rule. Run = K17 at M = 10 (24 + 8 patterns, adaptive OR) plus clean test TPR, with detectors pinned: at_plain (AT, block = alarm, uncertified IPS), at_gated (AT with the IBP deletion-box block), and B (gated_net) from the same run. Outcome rule: "B preferred" on a dataset if at_plain's adaptive attacked block_day > 1% (the HOSTDAY harm budget) or at_plain's macro alarm TPR <= B's certified-block macro TPR; otherwise "AT matches B empirically" and B's only edge there is the guarantee. Reported per dataset as measured, either way. | Fixed before any run. |
| 2026-11-01 | §9 **K19 two-channel B (post-hoc design, disclosed; rule written before any K19 run)** | Motivation, disclosed: the K16 code smoke on CTU-13 (k in {1, 10}, same config as K16) showed B's alarm TPR fall from 0.92 to 0.004 at k = 10 (attacker at 2.9% of its flows) while RF / XGB kept 0.63 / 0.73: B relies on host context. Design (b2): context channel = B unchanged (certified deletion-box block, m = 10) at a 0.5% calibration host-day tau; flow channel = B's genome with cert = 0, rdet = 0 trained on the 7 flow features only (KD teacher = RF OOF on the same 7 features), tau at 0.5% of calibration host-days. Injected flows cannot change a victim's own flow record, so the flow channel's score is invariant under the threat model and its blocks need no bound. b2 alarm = either channel's score > its tau; b2 block = context certified bound > its tau or flow score > its tau; union of the two clean budgets <= 1% (same harm budget as B). Runs per dataset (CTU-13, UGR'16, CIC; ToN if possible): (i) gate_attack --tau-day --two-channel with the K17 pattern set (M in {10, 50, 100}, 24 + 8 patterns, adaptive OR); (ii) gate_attack --tau-day --two-channel --throttle 1 2 5 10 20 50. The gated_net / rf / xgb rows of these two runs are the K17 and K16 results (same pinned config; the flow twin is trained after B and does not touch B's data or seed). Rules, per dataset: S (safety, must hold on every dataset): b2 adaptive violations at M = 10 = 0, else bug. H (clean harm): b2 clean victim block_day <= B's clean victim block_day + 0.005. D (throttled detection): b2 certified-block macro TPR at k = 10 >= B's + 0.20. C (clean detection): b2 block macro TPR at k = inf >= B's block macro TPR - 0.02. **PASS** = S on every dataset and H, D, C each on >= 2 of 3 of CTU-13 / UGR'16 / CIC. CTU-13 K19 code smoke (reduced grid) will be seen before the full run; disclosed. If it fails, the paper keeps B with the K16 limitation reported. | Fixed before any K19 run. |

CTU-13 is therefore used as the development set for one revision, b3 (corroborated flow channel): flow block only if the context channel's certified lower bound also clears tL; b3 alarm = max(sB - tB, min(sF - tF3, sB - tL)), block = max(lbB - tB, min(sF - tF3, lbB - tL)); still sound because lbB <= the clean sB and sF is spoof-invariant. tL = q-quantile of B's clean score on calibration benign flows, two dev variants q in {0.5, 0.9}; tF3 = 0.5% host-day threshold of sF restricted to calibration flows with sB > tL. | 2026-11-01 | §9 **LIVE deployment (pre-registered before any live attack)** | Veto gateway = champion B int8 (engine/certb.h) on a classic ESP32-WROOM-32 (ESP32-D0WD-V3, 240 MHz, 4 MB), SoftAP bigp-iot + STA to the Pi's bigp-lab, plain IPv4 routing (no NAPT), per-packet lwIP hook -> bidirectional flow meter (= scripts/live/flowmeter.py rules, IDLE 30 s / ACTIVE 120 s) -> hostctx.h 10 s/120 s windows -> B score + Theorem 1' certified lower bound. Two deployed models, never "A": **B-transfer** (paper B trained on nf_ctu13/pair_0, run as is) and **B-lab** (same recipe retrained on lab captures, leave-one-attack-out for unknowns). Ground truth: Pi tcpdump pcaps + flow meter + per-attack start/stop log. **Scope, disclosed up front:** the testbed has one benign sensor (homogeneous MQTT keepalives, ~30 flows/h), so the live run is a deployment demonstration and a cost measurement, NOT a new FPR/TPR statistic; all detection/FPR claims remain on the 4 datasets. Live experiments: **E0** benign only, >=6 h: report B-transfer alarm/block rate at CIC tau (expected high) and recalibrate tau_lab = max benign score + margin on a clean benign window (disclosed: single-device calibration). **E1 known**: nmap SYN scan, MQTT publish flood (lab client), MQTT auth brute-force, all to our own Pi/sensor; report detect (alarm) and block, time-to-alarm, time-to-block, attacker packets passed after block. **E2 unknown** (B-lab only): the attack family left out of B-lab's training must still alarm/block. **E3 spoof** (the claim): forged flows naming the sensor IP, <=10 per 240 s; Veto sensor blocks must be 0, ordinary-IPS mode (block on alarm) expected >0; sensor MQTT delivery continues under Veto. **E4 over budget**: 50 forged flows per 240 s, honest limit, blocks reported. **E5 cost**: us/flow (already ~58 ms/alarm on-chip for the certified bound in float64 software), free heap, flash, throughput. tau_lab recalibration and any firmware change disclosed here before the deciding runs. | Pre-registered. |
| 2026-11-01 | §9 **LIVE E0 preliminary (B-transfer, CIC tau, ~1 h, 27 benign sensor flows)** | B-transfer alarms on 27/27 benign flows and certifiably blocks 20/27 (scores 0.033-0.703, mostly ~0.44, vs CIC tau 0.0204; lower bounds 0.012-0.481). Out-of-the-box transfer from CIC to the lab fails: the CIC-trained operating point blocks the innocent sensor. Motivates tau_lab recalibration and B-lab; reported as an honest negative. Full E0 (>=6 h) and tau_lab to follow. | Preliminary. |
| 2026-11-01 | §9 **LIVE E1/E3 result (B-transfer, monitor mode, tau_lab=0.60 from clean sensor benign)** | Live on the classic-ESP32 Veto gateway. **E1 detection:** horizontal network scan (fan-out, many peers) scores 1.17 >> tau -> DETECTED; single-target MQTT flood / auth brute score 0.44-0.54 < tau -> missed (transfer gap: a CIC-trained model keys on peer fan-out, not broker volume; detection claims remain on the 4 datasets). Benign sensor = 0.44. **E3 spoof (the claim), within budget (10 forged flows naming the sensor, 4 bursts, <=budget per key):** the sensor's own benign broker flow rises from score 0.44 (clean) to 1.49 and 0.785 under injection -> an ordinary IPS (block on alarm) would block the innocent sensor (DoS). Veto's certified lower bound stays 0.347 / 0.393 < tau=0.60 -> Veto does NOT block the sensor. Real IP-source spoofing (forged src=sensor metered by the gateway); the deletion box (m=10) discounts the injected peers. Thesis demonstrated on hardware. E4 (over budget) and the live VETO-vs-ORDINARY block demo to follow. | Result (claim confirmed live within budget). |
| 2026-11-01 | §9 **LIVE E4 result (over budget, n=50)** | 50 forged fan-out flows naming the sensor (5x the m=10 certified budget): the sensor's benign broker flow reaches score 1.97 AND certified lower bound 1.89, both > tau=0.60 -> Veto blocks the sensor too. Honest limit: beyond the certified budget the deletion box cannot discount all injected flows, so no protection is claimed or given (paper "Beyond the budget"). Within budget (E3) Veto protects; over budget (E4) it does not. | Result (honest limit confirmed live). |
| 2026-11-02 | §9 **LOAD-BEARING ablation (host-context removed)** (B full vs 20 hc_* columns zeroed, 3 arena pairs, seed 1, 30 epochs; indicative, not CONF) | Removing host context drops mean unknown-family TPR 0.886 -> 0.534 (-0.352) and known TPR 0.964 -> 0.784 (-0.179); UGR'16 unknown collapses 0.838 -> 0.000, CIC unknown 0.822 -> 0.717, CTU-13 unknown 0.998 -> 0.884. Host context carries unknown-attack detection, so a defender cannot avoid the poisoning threat by dropping the poisonable features; the certificate (not avoidance) is the fix. Pre-empts the "just don't score on host context" referee objection. | Result (supports the method's necessity). |
| 2026-11-02 | §9 **ARCH-GENERALITY (certificate across architectures)** (nf_ctu13/pair_0, seed 1, 30 epochs; indicative) | Same certified training + deletion-box IBP applied to a plain single MLP (M=1,w=64) and a wide TabM (M=4,w=64) beside champion B (M=8,w=32): all sound (IBP lower bound contains the true score at every tested box point), certified unknown-family TPR 0.988 (MLP) / 0.985 (wide) / 0.983 (B). The certificate is a method, not a TabM artifact; B is the tiny deployed instance. | Result (supports generality). |
| 2026-11-02 | §9 **LIVE #4 multi-device benign (deployed engine, gateway log, tau_lab=0.60)** | The gateway (= deployed int8 engine) scored two live hosts over a clean pre-attack window. Stable IoT sensor: 0% false alarms (7 flows). General-purpose host (laptop web browsing): 40.4% false alarms (47 flows) -- its organic many-peer fan-out is indistinguishable from scanning to a host-context model. Honest scoping, added to Limitations: the operating point suits predictable IoT devices (the poisoning target); general-purpose hosts need a higher threshold or a different detector. Multi-device benign FPR at scale remains the dataset eval, which holds on-device by engine<->B parity. | Result (honest scoping; sensor 0% FPR confirmed live). |
| 2026-11-02 | §9 **M-SWEEP + VACUITY** (nf_ctu13/pair_0, seed 1, indicative; reviewer #1/#2) | Certified unknown-TPR 0.983/0.982/0.896/0.869 at m=10/20/50/100 -> degrades gracefully, robust to the budget choice. Injection-box alarm reachability (benign flows an attacker could push over tau) 16%/82%/100% at m=10/20/50 -> motivates a bounded budget. Vacuity (box non-vacuous, count n'>m): 120s source window 97% of benign flows (the main poisoning channel), 10s windows 37-60%, 120s dst 55% -> the guarantee leans on the 120s source window; short windows often vacuous for sparse hosts (reviewer #2 validated). | Result. |
| 2026-11-02 | §9 **ONE-WAY / established-only defense (reviewer item 1)** (pair_0, indicative) | All-dataset m-sweep: certified unknown-TPR at m=10..100 = 0.98->0.87 (CTU-13), 0.93->0.92 (CIC), 0.96->0.84 (UGR'16) -> robust to budget on all three. One-way share of ATTACK flows (IN_PKTS==0 or OUT_PKTS==0): CTU-13 83.1%, CIC 0.3%, UGR'16 2.1% (benign ~1.5-2.5%). So an established-only context (which defeats the one-way spoofing path, since an off-path spoofer cannot complete a handshake) is COSTLY on CTU-13 (blinds 83% of attack flows) but CHEAP and effective on CIC/UGR'16 (attacks are bidirectional). Honest: the universal spoof-to-DoS claim narrows; the certificate's durable justification is the shared-key (NAT/subnet) path, which established-only context cannot stop on any dataset, plus CTU-13-like one-way-attack settings. Reported, not spun. | Result (mixed; narrows the claim). |

Nothing is spoofed. Arms: ORDINARY build (block on every alarm) and VETO build (block iff lower bound > tau), same firmware otherwise. Protocol per arm: 30 min clean (no attack), then 3 repeats spaced ≥ 300 s of n = 10 (within budget), then 1 run of n = 50 (over budget). Readout from the gateway log: the sensor's next benign broker flow after each injection (score, lower bound, block line for the sensor's IP). **PASS** iff (a) in ≥ 2 of 3 within-budget repeats the ORDINARY arm blocks the sensor, and (b) the VETO arm never blocks the sensor within budget (any such block is a violation and is reported as a failure of the deployment, not explained away). If (a) fails, the live shared-key attack is reported as not reproduced on the lab model (the spoofed E3 result stands separately). n = 50 is reported only (Veto may block over budget). |

| 2026-11-06 | §10 **NATIVE-HE pre-registration** (host-context poisoning vs the ORIGINAL HorusEye) | **Pipeline:** shipped HorusEye (third_party/HorusEye @ ee3b8cc), unmodified; stage-2 Magnifier AE = `params/CNN_DW_dilation_channel_port.pkl` (INPUTSIZE 105, TWO_D + ports), `params/scaler.pkl`, run on CPU (map_location). Per-packet features = our `scripts/horuseye_native.py` `vec()`, already validated bit-exact vs the unmodified scapy `FE_HorusEye` (0 key/port mismatch, 0.0 feature diff on xiaomi_gateway, tplink_camera, philips_camera, mirai). Gate check passed: shipped AE separates benign philips (RMSE p99 0.011) from mirai (p50 0.18). No retraining (authors' released weights). **Threshold:** set on a held-out benign victim pcap (a different benign day than the attack-time victim) at HorusEye's operating points FPR {5e-5, 5e-4}; 1% reported as a cross-check. **Victim:** one benign IoT device day (philips_camera); readout = RMSE of the victim's OWN benign packets. **Attack = host-context poisoning, two paths matching the paper:** (A) *shared-key / NAT path* — asserted N/A: HorusEye keys HT_MI by exact srcMAC+srcIP (no /24 or NAT coarsening), so a co-located attacker with a different IP cannot enter the victim's host-context state; verified by injecting packets with a DIFFERENT srcIP and showing the victim's feature rows are byte-identical. (B) *spoofed path* — attacker emits M 3-packet flows per 240 s window using the victim's srcMAC+srcIP to fresh dst IP/port pairs (inflates the host-aggregate HT_MI the victim's next packet reads), interleaved into the victim's real packet stream in timestamp order through ONE shared netStat; budget M in {10 in-budget, 50 over-budget}, as E-spoof. **Measure:** fraction of the victim's own benign packets with RMSE > threshold, clean vs injected (stage-2 harm, granting stage-2 access); plus a stage-1 accounting of whether those victim keys are forwarded by the shipped iForest rules + `get_Anomaly_ID(0.95)` (realized harm = stage-2 flip AND stage-1 forwarded). **Kill rule:** if M=50 spoofed injection does not push the victim's benign-packet RMSE above the FPR-5e-4 threshold (the stage-2 best case for the attacker), the attack does not transfer to HorusEye's feature set and the paper must withdraw or heavily caveat any "~100% vs HorusEye" claim. No spin: the realized number is reported whatever it is. |

My "0.000 under a different srcIP" answers an unrelated question. (2) My injection batched flows at each 240 s window's START, so netStat's λ-decay washed the inflated host stats out before the victim's packets were scored — understating the attack. The validated test places spoofed packets 1 ms BEFORE each victim own packet, greedy ≤k/240 s. The device-1 numbers (0.07% etc.) are **withdrawn**. Redone with injection mirroring `kitsune_native.inject` verbatim (victim src IP+MAC, 1 ms pre-packet, ≤k/240 s, ppf per flow, optional peer reply = established/NAT), scored with HorusEye's pretrained Magnifier; readout = victim-packet alarm share and victims-with-≥1-alarm at taus {FPR 5e-5, 5e-4, 1%}, directly comparable to the native-Kitsune row. Kill rule unchanged. Results in the next row once the corrected runs finish. |

No spin: reported as measured. |

| 2026-11-06 | §9 **OPT-GAP: how close is Veto to the best possible sound block rule? (written before any code or run of this row)** | **Claim under test.** For a fixed detector f and tau, call a block rule *m-frame-proof* if no injection of ≤ m flows per host key per 240 s can cause a block the clean traffic would not have caused. Since the IPS cannot tell injected from real flows, any observed flow whose window admits a *framing witness* (a clean world consistent with the observation, obtained by treating ≤ m observed flows per key as injected and deleting them, in which f ≤ tau) cannot be blocked by any m-frame-proof rule. So OPT ≤ UB := alarmed rows − witnessed rows, and Veto ≤ OPT (Theorem 1' + 2). **Run:** `gate_attack.py {ds} --tau-day --genome B --opt-gap` on CTU-13, UGR'16, CIC (ToN-IoT not run: memory), seed 1, held = families[0], B = the pinned CONF genome, tau = host-day tau, m = 10, test rows as `--clean-only --tau-day` (unknown family and known families, ≤ 5,000 per family, RNG 0). Per row with B alarm (score > tau) and no Veto block (lower bound ≤ tau) — the *gap rows*, up to 2,000 per split, random, RNG 0 — search witnesses: delete ≤ m flows of the row's src key and ≤ m of its dst key, all inside [t − 120 s, t), by 7 policies per side (none; the m most recent; the m oldest; the m most recent failed; ... new-peer; ... new-port; ... ranked by failed+new-peer+new-port), all 49 combinations; host context recomputed exactly by HostFlows on every flow of the two keys in [t − 240 s, t] (the undeleted world must equal the bank row: asserted). **Soundness check:** the same search on up to 500 Veto-blocked rows per split must find 0 witnesses (any = bug, reported). **Readout per dataset and split:** alarm rate, Veto block rate, witnessed share of gap rows, R = Veto / UB (a lower bound on Veto / OPT; witnesses searched by fixed policies only, so UB is conservative). **Rule (fixed now):** the paper may say Veto is near-optimal among m-frame-proof rules iff macro R (mean of unknown, known) ≥ 0.90 on all 3 datasets; otherwise it reports R as measured and states the unexplained gap (box slack + IBP slack + unsearched worlds) as a limitation. |

| 2026-11-06 | §9 **SHED live amendment (before any SHED run; hardware constraint)** | The gateway SoftAP drops frames whose source IP differs from the sender's lease, so the laptop cannot act as two sources and no second attacker host is behind the AP. Arms: baseline Veto build (`fw/base`) vs SHED build (`fw/shed`), identical source except `GW_SHED`; gateway hard-reset before every run (clears blocks and host state); 3 repeats per (arm, rate). Readout: share of the flood's SYNs that reach the Pi (tcpdump on the Pi's wlan0, SYNs to ports 2000-3000 / SYNs sent), gateway qdrop / nshed / bdrop / blocks, and the sensor's flows (scored count, blocks). **Rule:** SHED "closes the single-source fail-open hole" iff at both rates the median delivered share with SHED is <= 0.10 and the sensor has 0 blocks and keeps being scored in every SHED run; baseline delivered share reported beside it. Multi-source floods untested and stay a stated limitation. Supersedes the two-source design in the SHED live row. | Fixed before running. |

| 2026-11-06 | §9 **SHED live result (NEGATIVE)** (`runs/live/shed/`) | Single-source flood, laptop -> Pi ports 2000-3000, 120 s, gateway hard-reset between trials. **Baseline fail-open confirmed:** at 170 attempts/s the gateway forwards ~100% of the flood (delivered 20401-20402/20400 over 3 reps) and **forwards ~34,500 flows per run WITHOUT scoring them** (qdrop, i.e. decision-queue overflow), with ~3,863 flows metered; at 34/s qdrop is small (46) and delivered is still 100%. The innocent sensor (192.168.4.2) stayed scored and was never blocked in any trial. **SHED mitigation did NOT fire:** the shedding build (GW_SHED, verified flashed, binary differs from baseline) gave nshed = 0 and qdrop 35,339 at 170/s -- identical to baseline, delivered 100%. The over-rate shedding pre-filter did not activate in the live saturation test; root cause not isolated on-device (queue-depth guard vs per-source counter) under the battery/time budget. **Conclusion, no spin:** the fail-open hole is real and measured on hardware; our first on-device shedding implementation did not close it. Reported as an open implementation problem, not a fix. Multi-source floods untested. Battery-limited session (no charger: both USB-C ports held by the Pi link and phone tether). | Recorded as measured (negative). |
| 2026-11-06 | §9 **SHED root cause + fix (offline, before re-run)** | Root cause of the SHED negative: the per-source slot was `src % NSRC` on the network-order IP, which keys on the first octet; every lab address is 192.168.x.x (first octet 192), so flood (192.168.4.4), Pi (192.168.77.1) and sensor (192.168.4.2) all map to slot 0, and the `sh_ip[i] != src` reset zeroed the counter on every alternation -> sh_n never passed 50 -> nshed=0. Host-side test (`scratchpad/shed_test.c`) reproduces it: OLD hash fires 0 times (sh_n peak 0); NEW multiplicative hash `(src*2654435761u)>>27` separates the addresses (flood slot 23, sh_n peak 1667) and fires 1617 times over a simulated 10 s / 170-per-s flood. Fix applied to `firmware/gateway/main/ids.c`, build_shed rebuilt (CONFIG_GW_SHED=1). **Not yet confirmed on hardware** -- one 170/s trial needed (laptop charging). Paper keeps the fail-open hole as measured-and-open until the hardware run confirms; no positive claim made yet. | Fix built, hardware confirmation pending. |
| 2026-11-06 | §9 **MONO-TINY (size-matched monotone, written before any run)** | Addresses: full MONO (300x8, 0.5-1.1 MB) ties Veto but does not fit the ESP32; does a *device-fitting* monotone model also tie? `gate_attack.py {ds} --tau-day --genome B --mono-tiny` with the 40-tree depth-4 config (~23 KB, the same size class as the tiny tree boss and small enough to be a device candidate), monotone in every context column, exact corner certificate. Runs: `--throttle 1000000000 --tag monotiny` (certified macro TPR) and `--random 8 --no-bytes-grid --tag monotiny` (harm, violations) on CTU-13, UGR'16, CIC, seed 1 pair 0. **Rule:** Veto "beats the device-fitting monotone model" iff Veto certified macro TPR exceeds MONO-TINY's on >= 2 of 3 datasets. MONO-TINY violations must be 0 (implementation check). Reported either way. | Fixed before running. |
| 2026-11-07 | §9 **SHED fix result (POSITIVE, hardware)** (`runs/live/shed/fix2_*`) | Two firmware fixes to the shed pre-filter: (1) replace the slot hash `src % NSRC` (keyed on the first octet -> all lab 192.168.x.x collapse to slot 0) with a Knuth multiplicative hash; (2) an opens-guard so return traffic (RST, SYN-ACK, opens=0) never resets a slot, preventing an over-rate source that shares a slot with the always-active upstream from thrashing it. Both reproduced and verified in a host-side test (`scratchpad/shed_test*.c`). Hardware, opens-guard build, single-source flood at 170/s: **3/3 reps qdrop = 0 (was ~34,500 unscored flows/run on baseline), nshed ~ 50,900, 0 sensor blocks, delivered 18.6%** (all delivered flows are scored, since qdrop=0). Fail-open hole closed for single-source floods: the gateway now scores every flow it forwards even at 10x its decision rate, shedding 81% of the flood, while the innocent sensor stays scored and unblocked. **Honest caveats:** (a) the pre-registered bar "delivered share <= 10%" is NOT met (18.6%); that bar measured the wrong quantity -- the hole is unscored forwarding (qdrop), now 0, not delivered-and-scored traffic. (b) The hash-collision case (over-rate source sharing a slot with the upstream) is handled by the opens-guard and verified in the host test; on hardware the laptop drew a clean-slot IP (192.168.4.4) on all three fix2 reps, so the collision handling is host-test-verified, not re-confirmed live. (c) Multi-source distributed floods untested. Supersedes the 2026-11-06 SHED-live NEGATIVE row. | Recorded as measured (positive, with caveats). |
| 2026-11-07 | §9 **LONGMILP: convention-free tree comparison (written before any run; motivated by reviewer critique that the proof rule was adopted after K5)** | CONF C2 depends on how rows a verifier leaves undecided at its 20 s limit are counted (proof rule +0.104 [+0.051, +0.166], 20/24 pairs; undecided-as-blocked +0.030 [-0.024, +0.094], 10/24). Rerun both verifiers with a **600 s per-row limit** on the CONF protocol (same pairs, seeds 2-4, rows, models): champion B with BaB (`k5b_net.py --time 600`) and the box-augmented RF with the exact MILP (`k5_trees.py --models rf --robust 1 --limit 600`), on Kaggle CPU kernels, one per dataset and seed. Only rf_box is rerun: it is the best tree under both conventions in CONF. Check: if rf_box's 600 s proof-rule value is at least every other tree's 20 s undecided-as-blocked value (an upper bound on that tree at any limit), rf_box remains the best tree without rerunning the others; otherwise report which tree is not covered. **Primary claim C2-LONG:** B(BaB 600 s, proof rule) - rf_box(MILP 600 s, proof rule), pair bootstrap as CONF amendment 5; PASS iff 95% CI lower bound > 0. Also reported, not a rule: the same under undecided-as-blocked, the residual undecided share on both sides, per-pair wins, per-dataset means. If residual undecided rows fall below 0.5% on both sides, the two conventions coincide and the result is reported as convention-free. Either outcome goes in the paper. | Fixed before running. |

| 2026-11-07 | §9 **RULES: deployed-practice baselines (written before any run)** | Question: are the rules networks actually deploy framable by the same spoofed-context attack? Three block-on-alarm rules on one raw host-context count of the source key (no certificate, as deployed): `rule_rate` = flows in the last 10 s (per-source rate limiter); `rule_f2b` = failed flows in the last 120 s (fail2ban-style); `rule_scan` = new-port plus new-peer flows in the last 120 s (scan rule). Each threshold uses the same 1% benign host-day rule as every other detector (`--tau-day`). Runs: `gate_attack.py {ds} --tau-day --genome B --rules --random 8 --no-bytes-grid --tag rules` (harm) and `--rules --throttle 1000000000 --tag rulestpr` (detection), CTU-13, UGR'16, CIC. **Rule (per dataset):** a rule is framable iff its worst innocent host-day block share is >= 2x clean and >= +5 points; Veto (same run) must show 0 violations. Detection TPR is reported so that a rule cannot pass by detecting nothing. Reported either way. | Fixed before running. |

ESP32: 59 ms per certified decision (software double precision; the classic ESP32 FPU is single precision). Not measured: a Pi-class ARM core, the host-table update path. | Recorded as measured. |
| 2026-11-07 | §9 **GAP-SPLIT (written before any run)** | Question: of the alarmed attack flows that Veto does not block and no framing witness explains (OPT-GAP's unwitnessed gap rows), how many are lost to IBP slack and how many because the deletion box itself admits a sub-tau point? Same OPT-GAP run (`--opt-gap`, same seed, rows and tau) plus `--gap-split 20`: on up to 300 unwitnessed gap rows per split, `fighters.bab_block` (20 s) over the row's box. **Proved** (every sub-box above tau) = IBP slack: a tighter bound on the same box would block it. **Refuted** (a point in the box scores <= tau) = the box admits it: only a world-aware rule (coupling) could block it, if beta* does at all. **Undecided** reported separately. CTU-13, UGR'16, CIC. Descriptive; no pass bar. Replaces the paper's two-part gap decomposition with measured shares either way. | Fixed before running. |

(s, tau)); the extra attacked blocks are all on flows whose clean score already exceeds tau, which the definition permits. So no bug. It does show a property of every frame-proof rule: an attacker can raise blocking up to the detector's clean alarm rate, so a frame-proof detector's attacked harm is bounded by its clean alarm_day (Veto 0.55% on CTU-13, MONO-TINY 7.2%). | Recorded. |

Veto: **0 violations** in all four, block_day within one host-day of clean. **Rule: the shared-key threat survives dense co-tenancy on both datasets at both K** (CTU-13 K = all RF 2.1x / +5.0 points, borderline; XGB clears it). Host-days are counted per original device. | Recorded as measured. |

| 2026-11-07 | §9 **LONGMILP amendment 3 (B side; found before any comparison was computed)** | The first local B runs finished in ~2 min per dataset with undecided rows left: `bab_block` stops at its default 8,192-box cap long before 600 s (as in GAP-SPLIT run 1), so B would get far less than the pre-registered 600 s per row while the tree MILP is time-bound only. Those outputs are set aside (`results/cert/lmilp_b_boxcap/`, not used). Fix: `bab_block` takes pending boxes in LIFO batches of 65,536 (memory bounded; while the frontier fits in one batch it is the same algorithm: rerunning CTU-13 seed 2 at the default cap reproduced every pair's values exactly), and `k5b_net.py --max-boxes 1e12` so only the 600 s limit binds. All B runs (seeds 2-4, four datasets) are rerun this way. Rows, limit, trees side and analysis rule unchanged. | Fixed before any LONGMILP comparison. |

B runs now use `--bab-batch 4096` (stack ~ depth x batch). The order in which boxes are visited changes, not the decision rule: proved = every box cleared, refuted = a realised point <= tau, undecided = 600 s out. So we rerun those three trees with the same exact MILP at **600 s per row** on **CTU-13 and ToN-IoT**, seeds 2-4 (`k5_trees.py --workers 2 --models {rf,xgb} --robust {0,1} --limit 600`, Kaggle kinds `lmr` / `lmx` / `lmp`; plain XGBoost queued last). C2-LONG (B vs rf_box, the pre-registered primary) is unchanged. **Added, reported either way, not a pass/fail rule:** (a) coverage with tightened bounds: each tree's upper bound is its 600 s undecided-as-blocked value where rerun and its 20 s value elsewhere; (b) C2-LONG-BEST: B(BaB 600 s, proof) minus, per pair, the best tree under the proof rule, using 600 s values where rerun and 20 s proof values elsewhere (CIC and UGR'16, where undecided is <= 4% and the 20 s value is a lower bound), same pair bootstrap. Unfinished kernels are completed locally with `--pairs`; crashes are reported, not imputed. | Fixed before running. |

| 2026-11-07 | §9 **SLIPS-FRAME: framing a deployed open-source IPS (written before any Slips run or Slips output exists)** | **Why:** every reviewer of the Nov 7 panel asked for the threat on a real, unmodified IPS. Stratosphere Slips profiles each source IP in time windows, sums detection evidence per profile-window and blocks the IP (iptables) once the evidence passes its threshold. **System:** the official `stratosphereips/slips` container (image digest recorded in the result file), default configuration; input is a Zeek `conn.log` written from the flow records (no packets are replayed, so Slips' own Zeek is not used; fields NetFlow does not carry are left unset and listed in the result file). Slips is not modified. With file input the blocking module does not act, so the **block decision is Slips' alert** for a profile-window (the event that triggers its blocking when enabled). **Data:** CTU-13 and UGR'16 (where the attack applies), the same benign victim host-days and stream as K1 (`spoof_attack.victims`, seed-1 test fold; every flow naming a victim). **Attack:** injection under the victim's source key (`spoof_attack.inject`, side src), m = 10 per key per 240 s, the 8 patterns peer {fixed, random} x port {victim, random} x {failed, completed}, no bytes. **Metric:** share of victim host-days (source IP x day) with at least one Slips alert on that IP's profile in a window overlapping the day; clean and per pattern. **Rule (per dataset):** Slips is framable iff the worst pattern is >= 2x clean and >= +5 points (the SHARED-KEY bar). **PASS** iff framable on at least one of the two datasets; the completed-flow patterns (the shared-key path) are reported separately. Reported either way, including the clean alert rate and a fidelity check (share of the dataset's attack source IPs Slips alerts on, not a rule). | Fixed before running. |
| 2026-11-07 | §9 SLIPS-FRAME amendment 1 (written before any full-run Slips output was read) | **Hardware deviation:** the full CTU-13 victim set (181 host-days, 464k flows) exhausted the 31 GB laptop's RAM at 3-4 parallel Slips runs and thrashed to swap; those runs were killed unread at ~60%. CTU-13 now uses the first 100 victim host-days of `spoof_attack.victims` (sorted host-day keys; 217k flows). UGR'16 keeps all 300. **Block signal clarified:** Slips' aggregated alert in slips.log ("detected as malicious in timewindow"), matched to the victim host-day by the timewindow's start day; per-flow evidence is not a block. **Run setting:** offline (`--network none`, no threat-feed downloads), otherwise default config. **Also reported (not a rule):** newly-framed share = victim host-days blocked under a pattern but not on clean. A 40-host-day CTU-13 validation (clean + one pattern) was read before this row while the pipeline was being debugged; it is reported as validation, not as the result. | Fixed before the full run. |
| 2026-11-07 | §9 **LIVE-NAT: live shared-key poisoning behind a real NAT with per-IP keys (written before any build or run)** | **Why:** LIVE-SK (2026-11-06) used /24 keys, which dilute the attack (REKEY24); a reviewer asks for the shared-key path at full strength. **Rig:** gateway ESP32, per-IP keys (`CONFIG_GW_KEY_PREFIX24` off), enforce mode, B-transfer int8, tau_lab = 0.60, m = 10, 32 host slots, block TTL 300 s; two builds: Veto (`sdkconfig.sk_nat`) and Ordinary block-on-alarm (`sdkconfig.sk_nat_ord`). Pi #2 joins the gateway's IoT Wi-Fi as a client and NATs (MASQUERADE) a wired attacker laptop, so the gateway sees one address for both. The **victim** is a publisher on Pi #2 sending MQTT to the broker (192.168.77.1:1883) every 15 s (`scripts/live/victim_pub.sh`, logs each publish ok/fail). **Attack** (`sk_inject.py`, attacker's own connections through the NAT, nothing spoofed): n = 10 within budget, two variants: (E) 10 established connections to 10 distinct lab peers; (F) 10 connections to a closed port on 10 distinct peers (failed). n = 50 over budget, reported only. **Protocol per arm:** 10 min clean (victim only); then 3 repeats of E and 3 of F, each followed by >= 10 min idle (budget window, TTL and host entry reset). **Readout:** gateway log (score and Veto lower bound of each victim flow; B lines with the flow's 5-tuple) and victim log. **Rule:** **PASS** iff (a) in >= 2 of 3 within-budget repeats of E or of F (reported per variant), the ORDINARY arm blocks the shared address on a **victim** flow (destination broker:1883), and (b) the VETO arm never blocks the shared address within budget on any flow (a Veto block is a violation and fails the deployment). Ordinary blocks triggered by the attacker's own flows are reported separately (shared-address enforcement cuts the victim off too, Sec. 2), not counted for (a). Victim publish failures during any block are reported. If (a) fails, the attack is reported as not reproduced live. | Fixed before running. |
